California Issues Report Reflecting Fathom's Recommendations For Strong Independent AI Oversight

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Fathom calls for speedy implementation as Newsom endorses mandatory IVO oversight for frontier AI

BERKELEY, Calif., Nov. 18, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Today, California Governor Gavin Newsom released a roadmap for implementing the state's newly-passed AI laws, reflecting many of recommendations Fathom made to the state on building out the first-ever independent verification organization (IVO) framework to oversee AI models and systems.

Fathom CEO and Co-Founder Andrew Freedman released the following statement:

"Governor Newsom's report is a detailed roadmap for how a government can quickly and effectively operationalize independent oversight of AI. It is also a full-throated endorsement of IVOs as the way to mitigate catastrophic risk.

In my opinion, this is the best a report like this can be. Conceptually strong with concrete next steps. Now, it's time to stop admiring the problem and move with all deliberate speed to execute on these solutions.

Given the rapidly advancing capabilities, this report should serve as the starting gun for action. There will be legitimate questions about how to turn this into legislative action and rulemaking. There will be debates about the power of the states and the power of the federal government. There will be disagreements over logistics, timing, and costs.

But the time for navel-gazing is over. We are eager to see the states and the federal government demand transparency, mandate that IVOs measure and address risk, and utilize traditional government powers to set meaningful guardrails on AI and prevent catastrophic harm. We will be pushing them to do so."

Fathom, the organization that designed and championed the IVO framework, had also detailed in its input to the state how IVOs can be used to address key AI governance challenges, including monitoring for recursive self improvement (RSI) and mitigating catastrophic risk. Many of Fathom's recommendations are included in the state's report:

California passed the first-ever IVO framework into law this summer via SB 813 and AB 1405, with Governor Newsom accelerating its implementation in a subsequent executive order, and naming Fathom Senior Advisor Dr. Gillian Hadfield to his panel of AI experts.

About Fathom: Fathom is an independent nonprofit whose mission is to build a governance architecture that helps society navigate the transition to a world with AI by fostering trust, safety, and innovation. Fathom has developed and championed the independent verification model for AI and works with policymakers across the country to put it into practice. Learn more at http://fathom.org.

RDJ Had Made His ‘Peace’ With His MCU Exit: ‘It’s Really Hard To Explain The Love I Have For This’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

I Can’t Get Over Patrick Stewart And Ian McKellen’s Reactions When Two Avengers Were Caught Staring On Doomsday

I Just Found Out How Robert Downey Jr. Could’ve Returned To The MCU Before Avengers: Doomsday

Anthony Mackie Defends His Viral Comments About Vision And Doomsday (But Do We Believe Him?)

I don’t know about you, but my curiosity about Robert Downey Jr. in Avengers: Doomsday just keeps growing. Even though we know he’s under the mask of Doctor Doom, there’s a lot we still don’t know about his role in the newest upcoming Marvel movie. Now, with just a month between us and Doomsday, RDJ got real about where he was at with the MCU before he got the call to join it again.

Downey was revealed to be Doomsday’s Victor von Doom in the summer of 2024, during Marvel’s Hall H panel at San Diego Comic-Con. It was a huge surprise not only to fans but to the actor, too. As the Oscar winner shared:

I kind of made my peace with it, and it felt so definitive by the time Endgame had been released. When it was first broached by Kevin [Feige], who said, ‘Hey, do you think you would ever?’ And then, I really just thought, ‘How many surprises are left?’

When Avengers: Endgame came out in 2019, it was the end of an era for the MCU. It felt like the franchise had found a great way to wrap up the arc of Tony Stark/Iron Man by having him be the one to save the universe from Thanos. As RDJ said in a new Marvel video, he had made his “peace” with no longer being part of the MCU. But then Marvel president Kevin Feige called.

Recasting the same actor in the same franchise in a different role is pretty unheard of, unless you’re the MCU. It’s the ultimate big move for The Multiverse Saga, and clearly they convinced the actor to rejoin the Marvel family in a whole new way. As Downey Jr. continued:

Any of these movies is only as good as its villain. Victor is a master manipulator, and because of that, there’s levels to the stakes here. It’s such a long bench of talent. These people are people that I would do any movie with eight days a week. We just really keep trying to earn it with every breath. It’s really hard to explain the love I have for this.

Aww, this is sweet. I mean, it’s quite the honor to be asked to play a Marvel villain, especially when it’s one that will assemble just about every character in the universe. It’s wild when you think about it, because the villain that was originally being teased as the big bad of this saga was Kang. However, after Jonathan Majors got caught in some legal trouble, Marvel made a pivot. However, according to recent words from Feige, Doctor Doom was allegedly always in the cards.

And let’s not forget that Chris Evans’ Steve Rogers is also set to come back alongside the likes of Chris Hemsworth’s Thor, Paul Rudd’s Ant-Man, Anthony Mackie’s Sam Wilson, Tom Hiddleston’s Loki, and the casts of Fantastic Four: First Steps, Thunderbolts* and the original X-Men movies.

RDJ’s comments certainly make me wonder what we don’t know about Doctor Doom. And I can't wait to learn more about him when Avengers: Doomsday hits theaters on December 18. While we wait, you can catch up on all the Marvel movies and TV shows thus far with a Disney+ subscription.

Rockstar's budget doesn't "represent a business model anyone else can or probably should emulate"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

It's pretty much impossible to describe GTA 6 as anything other than a massive success. It is expected to break commercial records set by GTA 5 and it's tied with only The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time as the highest rated game of all time on Metacritic. And yet, because we're living in 2026 after all, many Rockstar developers who helped bring the game to life are now worrying about losing their jobs.

Writing for The Guardian, Rockstar animation programmer Nestor Uriart argues in support of this month's UK-wide Rockstar Games union-recognition ballot, believing unionization is key to "safeguard our jobs."

Games like Battlefield 6, and the layoffs that happened after its commercially successful launch, prove time and time again that strong sales and reviews are nothing compared to shareholder value when it comes to deciding whether or not to implement mass layoffs at a studio backed by a publicly traded company. And yet, you'd think having your name attached to the literal biggest game of all time would get you some sense of job security, but apparently that's not the case for everyone at Rockstar.

"While the highs of a game release like this can be incredible, game workers have also come to expect other, less welcome side effects: intense overtime in the buildup and mass redundancies in the aftermath," writes Uriart, arguing that unionizing could also help address allegations Rockstar has faced in the past of crunch culture. "With a strong union we could make sure there is no return to this system. We have already seen the introduction of some overtime pay, and now we want to keep on pushing to ensure that every hour worked in our industry is an hour paid."

Uriart says there's been "no indication that layoffs are coming, but it’s hard not to worry about what the future holds. Studios across the industry have been slashing huge portions of their workforces, with an estimated 24,000 roles axed globally over the last two years.

"It says a lot about the industry that even my colleagues and I, working on the biggest game of all time, are worried about getting laid off. But the truth is that workers across the sector are helpless to resist the waves of redundancies crashing around us, until we get organised."

As GTA 6 is just releasing this week, it's impossible to get a picture of the scale of its commercial success. We already know it's the most pre-ordered game of all time, but that was almost a given. Perhaps I'm being naive, but I would like to think it's going to be so overwhelmingly successful that Take-Two holds off on making any large-scale staffing changes. That said, now that development is transitioning into post-launch support, it's reasonable as a developer on the team to worry about downsizing.

Japan bond yields surge to 3.75%, hiding a quiet Bitcoin trap

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Demand for Japan’s 20-year bonds fell sharply as yields rose, putting yen-funded Bitcoin trades under scrutiny.

Demand fell at Japan’s Nov. 18 auction of a 20-year government bond, with investors offering to buy 3.64 times the amount the Finance Ministry accepted in the competitive auction, putting pressure on traders who borrow yen to hold Bitcoin.

The average accepted yield rose, while the yen barely moved between the Bank of Japan’s two 5 p.m. Tokyo readings.

In the Nov. 18 results, the ministry recorded ¥1.914 trillion in competitive tenders for issue 198 and accepted ¥525.8 billion. At the October auction, it received ¥3.139 trillion and accepted ¥537.1 billion.

Demand fell by much more than the amount sold in the competitive auction, and the average accepted yield rose 3.3 basis points to 3.756% from 3.723%.

Competitive coverage fell from 5.84× on Oct. 20 to 3.64× on Nov. 18 for Japan’s 20-year issue 198.

Source: Finance Ministry, Oct. 20 result · Finance Ministry, Nov. 18 result

At 5 p.m. in Tokyo, the midpoint of the Bank of Japan’s dollar-yen quote was 159.35 yen per dollar on Nov. 18, up one hundredth of a yen from 159.34 on Nov. 17.

Money-market broker Tokyo Tanshi reported a weighted average unsecured overnight call rate of 1.227%, unchanged from the previous day. The rate measures one-day borrowing in Japan’s call money market, for which the Bank of Japan publishes a daily series.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

The currency and the overnight rate track different parts of a yen-funded trade.

The dollar-yen midpoint moved one sen between readings. The overnight call rate was unchanged.

FX figures are 5 p.m. snapshots. The overnight figure is a broker benchmark. The two readings do not identify funding terms or exposures for individual Bitcoin positions.

The exchange-rate figures are midpoints of the Bank of Japan’s 5 p.m. Tokyo dollar-yen quote ranges. The overnight rate is Tokyo Tanshi’s weighted average for Nov. 18, compared with its reported previous-day change.

FX figures are 5 p.m. snapshots. The overnight figure is a broker benchmark. The two readings do not identify funding terms or exposures for individual Bitcoin positions.

A trader borrowing yen short-term to hold Bitcoin faces higher costs to renew the loan if funding rates rise. If the trader sells Bitcoin for dollars and leaves the yen debt unhedged, a stronger yen makes repaying the loan in dollar terms more expensive.

Bank for International Settlements researchers documented that feedback during the August 2024 unwind in leveraged currency and equity trades.

Bitfinex reported that aggregate Bitcoin futures open interest fell to 645,750 BTC over Nov. 14 and 15, 8% below Nov. 11. The futures total shows how much exposure was outstanding but gives no breakdown by funding currency.

BIS research showed that although it identified $271 billion in yen-denominated bank loans to non-bank borrowers outside Japan in the first quarter of 2024, it could not determine how much was used for carry trades.

The Finance Ministry plans to offer about ¥300 billion at the Nov. 25 reopening of a 40-year bond, the next test of long-dated demand.

A stronger signal for Bitcoin would be rising short-term yen borrowing costs or a sustained strengthening of the yen, alongside shrinking futures exposure and evidence that traders cutting positions had yen liabilities.

Siri Modular Apple Watch face gains two new styles with watchOS 27.3

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Siri Modular, the new Apple Watch face in watchOS 27, is gaining new style options. watchOS 27.3, currently in beta, adds two more style options to the existing set. This gives users five options for adjusting what type of information appears on Siri Modular.

Siri Modular originally shipped with three style options. Each supports a small line complication above the time.

Additionally, the original three styles include these options:

In watchOS 27.3, Apple introduces two new Siri features that work with Apple Watch Series 12 and Apple Watch Ultra 4.

First, there’s Siri Recap which uses the Apple Watch to turn audio into high-level text summaries. Second, there’s Live Rewind which lets you view a transcript of the last 15 seconds of spoken word.

The two new Siri Modular Apple Watch face styles help surface three things: invoking Siri, viewing conversations with Siri, and viewing Siri Recaps on the Apple Watch.

The new styles include a pill-shaped module that includes icon shortcuts that go directly to Siri conversations, invoke Siri AI, or go directly to Siri Recaps in the Siri app.

One style features a large digital clock with the new three-button Siri module. Another includes a compact digital clock with a Smart Stack tile and the new three-button Siri module.

This brings the style options to five. If you use the three circular complications configuration, you can also assign a Siri icon that invokes Siri AI.

watchOS 27.3 is currently in beta and expected to be released officially in December.

Van Gogh painting sells for record $190.4m after row over its Nazi history

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Sotheby's staff handle the "ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs" painting by Vincent van Gogh at an auction preview in Hong Kong last month

Auction house Sotheby's has sold a painting by Vincent van Gogh for a record $190.4m (Â£144.8m).

The sale of ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs ("Chestnut Trees in Blossom") shatters the renowned artist's previous auction record of $117.2 million set in 2022.

The historic sale in New York on Wednesday comes after an eleventh-hour agreement was reached to resolve a dispute regarding claims the art piece was previously sold under duress during the rise of the Nazis.

ChÃ¢taigniers en fleurs was painted in 1890 in the final two months of Van Gogh's life. It is considered one of the most important works of art from that period to remain in private hands, according to Sotheby's.

Sotheby's auction of $180m Van Gogh challenged over claims it was 'sold under duress'

It was not immediately clear who purchased the painting. The BBC has contacted Sotheby's for comment.

The oil painting, which was estimated to carry a value of $180m (Â£136m) prior to the sale, was put up for auction as part of a set called the Blaquier-Arrieta Collection.

Sotheby's announced in the hours before the auction that it had resolved a dispute between the Blaquier-Arrieta family and the heirs of the Fischer family - the prior German owners of the work - regarding the sale.

Representatives acting for heirs of the Fischer family had claimed the painting may have been sold under duress during the rise of the Nazis.

A lawyer for the family's heirs reportedly wrote to Sotheby's on their behalf, telling the auction house that the painting was sold in Amsterdam before World War Two because of financial losses the Fischer family's publishing company suffered under the Nazis. The BBC has contacted the lawyer for comment.

"In recognition of the painting's complex Nazi-era history and the suffering endured by the Fischer family during the Holocaust, the two families have reached a mutually satisfactory resolution and the work will be sold pursuant to their agreement," Sotheby's said on Wednesday.

Sotheby's website details the provenance of the oil painting and how it has researched the artwork changing hands since 1890.

It says Samuel Fischer - a famous German publisher - acquired it 1909 before it was passed down in his family after his death in 1934.

The painting was deposited with Paul Cassirer in Amsterdam in 1932 or 1933 before it went to Henriette and Marcel Kapferer in Paris in 1936, Sotheby's said.

The Blaquier family acquired it in 1975 and owned it for more than half a century before the latest sale, the auction house said.

How GTA 6 Is Leaning Into Advanced Physics For More Realistic Car Crashes

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Has this crazy timeline finally found balance? We can't know, but "Grand Theft Auto 6" is actually an honest-to-goodness playable game now. Sure, the longtime PC-only "BeamNG" became available on PlayStation 5 a month before GTA 6, but those "We got X before GTA 6" jokes can finally be put to rest. Thanks for 13 years of meme-ories.

With GTA 6, you'll become the ultimate Florida Man, as the gameplay actually extends beyond Miami-esque Vice City and into Rockstar Games' equally fantastic renderings of the Everglades, the Florida Keys, and elsewhere. For something escapist, though, GTA 6 is unapologetically realistic. Not only do vehicles in GTA 6 require fill-ups, your characters will even be tracked via Flock-like cameras. Factor in the realistic-looking crashes and you might just end up forgetting you're in-game and calling 911. Okay, maybe it's not quite that immersive, but the much-improved physics highlighted in vehicle collisions certainly add to the overall hyper-realistic visual effects.

There's no shortage of car racing games and driving simulators available at our fingertips. But as fun as, say, the Mario Kart franchise is, racing as a cow while riding a boombox with wheels is hardly ideal, let alone realistic. Don't even bother asking about crumple zones. Showcasing soft-body-style dynamics, however, adds so much more to the gameplay and is an impressive feat all its own.

When it comes to simulations, particularly collisions, how the vehicles crash and come apart generally falls into two categories: rigid body and soft body. As the names suggest, rigid bodies don't deform or change shape on-screen, no matter how severe the impact. With soft body physics, the causes and effects are shown. Sheet metal is compressed and contorts in a collision, for example.

With driving games, "BeamNG" was at the forefront and even shares a simple explanation of how it developed its soft-body physics simulation software model. For GTA 6, the driving dynamics and subsequent physics are previewed in the video game's trailers, especially in the mature-content extended version. While they may not be the same soft-body physics that "BeamNG" uses, the visual effects come close.

During chase scenes, bullets damage vehicles as they would in real life. You'll see tires blowing out, bullets ricocheting off grilles, and holes left in door panels. In other scenes, car windows actually shatter when smashed, fuel tanks rupture, and body panels deform. Other notable visual effects include functional dash gauges as well as dust and dirt that kick up as cars pull away.

Most impressive is that all of this is happening in real time during actual gameplay — these scenes aren't all preset. GTA BOOM reported that former Rockstar Games animator Mike York described how two different simulator systems can operate simultaneously through Rockstar's RAGE engine. Movements, impacts, and damage are calculated as they happen while thousands of physics computations per second occur in the background.

It's the little things that you might not have noticed, but now can appreciate. But did we have to wait so long? Well, at least we're not having to reenlist in the Army.

Why Bullish Stock Crushed it on Wednesday

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It topped analyst estimates in its latest reported quarter.

By Eric Volkman – Nov 18, 2026 at 7:43PM EST

The investing world was very bullish on cryptocurrency exchange operator Bullish (BLSH +5.66%) on Hump Day. That's mainly because the company posted an earnings report featuring a massive leap in headline profitability. Mr. Market rewarded this by sending Bullish's shares up by almost 6% in that trading session.

Before market open, Bullish, perhaps best known for owning the popular crypto news and analysis web portal CoinDesk, reported that its non-IFRS (adjusted) revenue in the third quarter was $84.4 million. That was up a sturdy 10% year over year, and was well above the average analyst estimate of $79.3 million.

On the bottom line, the company's net income swelled to over $576 million ($3.57 per share) from $18.5 million in the third quarter of 2025.

It was the vagaries of the crypto market that juiced Bullish's profitability. The value of its proprietary digital asset holdings flipped from an $11.4 million loss in the year-ago quarter to a $598.4 million gain. Stripping that and other one-time and unique items out of the equation, the company's adjusted net profit still recorded a gain, coming in at $18.7 million ($0.12 per share) from $13.8 million. Analysts were modeling $0.08 per share for the metric.

In the earnings release, Bullish announced the launch of a $300 million share repurchase program. It did not specify an end date for the initiative.

In the release, CEO Tom Farley signaled Bullish's strategy to grow its business via its pending acquisition. In May, the company agreed to acquire shareholder services company Equiniti in a deal valued at $4.2 billion; the transaction is expected to close this coming January. Farley aims to combine that company’s sprawling public-company shareholder registry with Bullish’s platform to offer tokenized stock trading.

I like that Bullish is active and assertive in building out its operations, and it's admirable that management is seeking ways to distinguish it in the crypto services sphere. That said, I'm not convinced tokenized securities will be a powerful motor of growth, so I'd temper my enthusiasm about its third-quarter showing.

Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella explains why he asked 60K employees to quit

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Microsoft CEO Satya Nadella explained Wednesday why he offered buyouts to 60,000 of his employees in October, essentially urging them to voluntarily quit instead of reducing the workforce via another massive layoff.

Short answer: AI, but not, he insists, in the way you might expect. He’s not saying he’s replacing employees with AI, especially software engineers. He’s saying he’s reorganizing Microsoft into a company where everyone uses AI to do their work, and their jobs become focused on managing that AI.

The buyout was “to make sure that at least everyone felt like they had agency in making choices,” he told Andrew Ross Sorkin during the DealBook Summit.

“Let’s take software engineering. We went from, ‘Well, we don’t buy that AI can ever do software.’ To suddenly, ‘Wow, it can do all software, and why do we need software engineers?’ To now rediscovering that software engineering is very much alive and, in fact, is going to thrive, except the way the team is organized and the work that a software engineer does has now got a new operating point,” he explained.

For some people, “that reorganization and change is sort of hard, right?” he added, “but, I mean, it has to be done.”

He offered the example of the team that builds Cowork, the AI agent in Microsoft 365 apps. He said the team consists of just 11 developers who have mastered using AI to do their jobs. “They build a software factory that builds the product. They don’t build the product.”

He believes that every employee will one day similarly become a manager of agents, rather than a person directly doing the work. “That level of direction is what, by the way, is going to happen in all jobs.”

But Microsoft, as a 51-year-old company, didn’t grow up as an AI-first company. “So the question is, how quickly can an incumbent transform the work, the workflow, and the work artifact, and with all the wage support that goes with it?” he said. “It’s going to take a bit more time than anyone expects. And there is going to be in all of this, you know, people who may say, ‘Hey, I have some other choices I want to make.’”

When asked about this same topic — why ask 60,000 people to voluntarily resign — last week by Gergely Orosz on his The Pragmatic Engineer podcast, Nadella further explained, “We have created enormous anxiety in people sort of feeling that they don’t have control of their own destiny because the way we have talked about this AI revolution.”

He called out talk about the singularity (the hypothetical point at which AI surpasses human intelligence) and predictions that AI will take away all jobs. “All of that leads anyone who even wants to change to be sort of anxious … You have to sort of walk the hard walk of really making the structural changes, getting people slotted, giving them opportunity.”

In other words, the buyout was the message to employees: You can get with the new AI-first program, which will radically change your job, or you choose to get on with your life. Elsewhere.

Elon Musk says: ‘I’m not MAGA’

📅 Nov 19, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elon Musk, who spent $277 million to get Donald Trump reelected president in 2024 and who, during that campaign, declared himself “dark MAGA” and who, after the election, was subsequently photographed sporting a cartoonishly large MAGA hat while visiting the Oval Office, has some news for Americans: None of that necessarily makes him MAGA.

“I’m not MAGA,” Musk declared Wednesday during a conversation with Andrew Ross Sorkin at The New York Times DealBook Summit. Musk joined the conversation via video call, as he is currently in China, joining President Trump for the APEC summit.

“Well, at least I’m not like … I’m not sure what MAGA means,” he backtracked. “If that means sort of blind adherence to a set of policies by the Republican Party, then I certainly don’t have that.”

The comments were made amidst a broader question about how Musk felt in the wake of this year’s midterms, during which a “blue wave” led to Democratic victories throughout the country. Musk, notably, spent a gargantuan amount of money (reportedly some $445 million) to support Republican candidates. Musk admitted Wednesday that such vast political spending might not have been a good use of his money.

“I feel pretty foolish about having spent so much money and not seen much for it,” he said. “Or maybe it would have been much worse without that. But it certainly shows that, you know, American elections are not just about money.”

During his conversation with Sorkin, Musk also seemed to lean away from his viral edgelord persona and claimed that he held no extreme views. He said: “I don’t think I have like, you know, any kind of extreme political views.”

Sorkin pushed Musk on this a little, noting that some people found his views on immigration extreme, to which Musk said: “Like what?” Mild laughter spread throughout the audience.

Ironically, the last time Musk spoke at DealBook, in 2023, he was in the middle of a very public battle with his social media site X’s advertisers. Critics had accused Musk of platforming a typhoon of hateful content on his site, and advertisers were threatening to walk over it. In response, Musk famously told Sorkin that he hoped those advertisers would “go f—” themselves.

Now Musk has an entirely new set of concerns. Democratic lawmakers in various committees have signaled that they may investigate Musk and his business activities as they probe potential corruption throughout the Trump administration.

Specifically, officials have said they’re interested in scrutinizing Musk’s role in DOGE, the “government efficiency” initiative that Musk launched inside the White House at the start of Trump’s new term, as well as Starlink’s activities in Ukraine, where the company has been accused of throttling the military’s access to its internet service.

Sorkin broached some of the potential concerns for the Tesla CEO: “You hear people like the chairman of the oversight committee saying, ‘Lawyer up,’ and you hear the president saying, ‘If I have to give him a pardon’ — meaning you — ‘I’ll give him a pardon.’”

Musk replied: “I don’t think I need a pardon by the way, because I haven’t committed any crimes.”

Musk then made one of those controversial statements he’s known for, appearing to compare an incoming Texas official to Lavrentiy Beria, the top head of the Soviet secret police under Josef Stalin.

“The incoming Texas, you know, House Majority Leader, said that I should go to prison,” Musk said. “I’m like, the guy’s trying to, you know channel Beria from, like, Stalin, where it’s, you know, show me the man, I’ll show you the crime type of thing.”

It’s not clear who (or what) Musk was talking about. Texas doesn’t have a House Majority Leader. However, he may have been referencing Gene Wu, who is the incoming chair of the Texas House Democratic caucus and who has suggested that Musk be investigated for potential criminal activity.

“Hopefully, in this country, you still need to do an actual crime before you go to prison,” Musk added.

Fathom Urges Rapid Implementation of California’s AI Oversight Plan

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

AI governance group Fathom said on November 18, 2026 that Governor Gavin Newsom’s new frontier AI roadmap reflected many of its recommendations, and urged California to implement mandatory independent evaluations for catastrophic risks quickly.

Fathom developed and advocated the independent verification organization (IVO) model, under which outside evaluators assess AI models and systems for risk. CEO and co-founder Andrew Freedman described the report as a concrete plan for putting independent oversight into practice and urged movement with “all deliberate speed.” He listed legislation, rulemaking, state-versus-federal authority, logistics, timing and costs as unresolved questions, and said Fathom would press state and federal governments for transparency, mandatory IVO risk measurement and government-enforced guardrails.

The California Government Operations Agency’s (GovOps) report, dated November 16, 2026 and released publicly on November 18, recommends legislative changes rather than imposing requirements itself. Its broader package includes baseline standards, emergency shutdown authority and possible limits on recursive self-improvement, building on SB 813’s IVO framework and AB 1405’s AI-auditor registry.

Its central oversight recommendation would require every large frontier AI developer to undergo continuing reviews by licensed IVOs, replacing the current voluntary approach for those developers. GovOps would license and continuously supervise the evaluators, with authority to revoke licenses when they lack sufficient expertise, independence or rigor. IVOs would submit unredacted assessments to the state, which would decide whether corrective action was warranted after engaging with the developer.

On both the division of responsibility and the legal test, Fathom says the report tracks its October 30, 2026 implementation memo. California would define acceptable safety outcomes in law, while IVOs would develop and update the technical methods and tests used to assess compliance. The proposed standard asks whether a frontier model is “unlikely to increase the risk of catastrophic harm above the level that would exist absent the model” and whether its anticipated benefits outweigh the residual risk. The design is meant to let testing methods change with the technology while preserving the state’s statutory outcome.

Recursive self-improvement (RSI), the point at which automated AI development becomes self-sustaining, is another area of overlap. The report would make verification of automated AI research and development metrics and assessment of the growing risks an initial IVO responsibility. Fathom’s memo also sought scrutiny of internal and external use, with pre-registered capability thresholds triggering state notification and a hold on further internal scaling pending reassessment.

The memo proposes phasing in each mandate once GovOps determines that designated evaluators have sufficient capacity and expertise in the relevant risk area, arguing that a dependable expectation of mandatory work would attract the capital and talent needed to expand independent verification instead of waiting for a mature market across every risk.

Initially, the state would assign an IVO from a predesignated pool, while the developer paid directly on a time-and-materials basis at state-benchmarked rates. Developer choice would expand in later phases as the pool deepened, but the state would retain powers to veto selections, require rotation, cap concentration, appoint additional assessors and decertify evaluators that lack rigor or independence. Payments would not depend on an assessment’s outcome.

Embedded evaluators would receive continuous access to necessary records, personnel and systems, including access for physical presence at defined decision points, backed by statutory confidentiality and anti-retaliation protections. Enforcement would remain with the state: the Attorney General could seek injunctions and civil penalties, while IVOs would make findings, prescribe corrective measures and refer imminent risks rather than seeking injunctions themselves or making their findings self-executing.

The practical test now sits with the Legislature and GovOps: converting the recommendations into binding duties while developing enough licensed evaluators and preserving credible state enforcement. Fathom’s design makes those steps interdependent, because each mandate depends on available expertise and an IVO’s findings require state action before they can carry coercive consequences.

Did Christopher Nolan Just Bring 4K Blu-Ray Discs Back to Stores?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

The Odyssey hit the shelves of Best Buy, just in time for the holidays.

Physical media lovers, take note: Best Buy has begun selling 4K Ultra HD Blu-ray discs in-store and online. If you’ve been paying attention, you’ll know how big a pivot this is for the electronics retailer. Back in 2023, the company announced it would cease all sales of DVDs and Blu-rays entirely.

Now, thanks to Christopher Nolan’s blockbuster phenom and Oscar frontrunner The Odyssey, which is the first-ever movie to be filmed entirely on IMAX cameras, the company is once again stocking its shelves with Blu-rays. Right now, you’ll find only copies of the Greek epic in store (with a small selection of other titles available for purchase directly through the retailer’s online store), and that makes sense considering how popular it was in theaters and how awesome it will look on big TVs at home.

But this move also raises the question: Will other 4K and standard Blu-rays be coming to Best Buy stores in the near future?

A Best Buy spokesperson didn’t immediately reply to our request for comment.

When the retailer first announced it would be ditching DVD and Blu-ray sales, it cited the decades-long decline in physical media sales — which fell from its peak revenue benchmark of $16 billion in 2005 to less than $1 billion in 2024 — and planned to populate the store space with other tech-based products.

The Odyssey, the nearly 3-hour-long big-screen adaptation of Homer’s epic poem, follows King Odysseus (played by Matt Damon) as he leads his soldiers on a 10-year journey home after the Trojan War. It features a stacked slate of acting talent from Anne Hathaway and Tom Holland to Zendaya, Jon Bernthal and Charlize Theron, and earned $1.8 billion at the global box office.

Those details, along with the timing of the coming holiday shopping season, have apparently inspired Best Buy to change course.

It should be noted that physical media still has value for many people. In an era when movies and TV shows (purchased or otherwise) are suddenly pulled from streamers, the allure of owning titles on DVD and Blu-ray endures. Not to mention, 4K Blu-rays offer better picture quality than their compressed, streaming versions.

Google’s AI Controls Have Major Districts Rethinking Search

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The New York Times reported on November 18, 2026, that New York City, Los Angeles and Chicago school officials are considering replacements for Google Search because Google’s coming school controls tie Search access to its AI features, in a report carried by The Verge.

The control change due in 2027 links the two settings: a district that turns off Gemini-powered features will turn off Search as well. Google education vice president Chris Phillips said in comments carried by the report that Search and AI are deeply integrated and that schools turning off the AI features would not retain access.

New York City schools chancellor Kamar H. Samuels said in a statement carried by the report: “If Google doesn’t reconsider their position, we will explore other search options.” Chicago Public Schools also said it would consider other providers if Google could not supply Search without AI.

Google’s announcement on November 3, 2026 set out the control changes and promised more details before rollout. Under the plan, K-12 student accounts will have Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom and Google Search switched off unless administrators explicitly enable them.

In a statement to The Verge, Google spokesperson Laura Breen described the 2027 controls as a response to educator feedback and said Google hears concerns about students using AI to shortcut learning while sharing the goal of helping students learn.

Google’s educational case for the integration rests on Guided Learning, which the November 3 announcement describes as a way to keep students actively challenged without overwhelming them. It uses open-ended questions and step-by-step breakdowns rather than immediately supplying final answers.

When Search identifies certain kinds of homework queries, it will prompt students to reason through the material step by step rather than display a solution outright. Google said it would pilot the Search version in select U.S. schools later in November 2026 to gather educator feedback before expanding to other districts over the following months.

Guided Learning also became globally available in Google Classroom that day. Activities there are anchored in teacher-assigned class material, and teachers receive real-time insights to identify where a class may need extra support.

In a release issued November 13, 2026, New York City Council Speaker Julie Menin and Council Member Shekar Krishnan said they had written to Google CEO Sundar Pichai and requested written answers by November 19, 2026. Krishnan chairs the council’s Committee on Oversight and Investigations.

Mayor Zohran Mamdani announced a one-year moratorium in September 2026 on AI use by public-school students from 2-K through eighth grade. The council said Google’s guidance puts those students at risk of losing Search, turning an AI restriction into a choice about access to a basic research tool.

The letter asks whether students will retain Search without opting into AI Mode, AI Overviews or any student-facing generative AI, and why Google cannot offer a student version that excludes those features. It points to YouTube Kids as an example of Google modifying a product for younger users through different features and content controls.

It also asks what safeguards would preserve genuine district choice and how Google measures, monitors and mitigates AI-generated misinformation in educational settings. On privacy, the council asks whether Gemini and other classroom AI products collect student data, what the data is used for, where it is stored and whether Google earns revenue from selling or using it.

Google’s AI in Education report says schools retain ownership of their data and that, within core Workspace for Education services, student data is not sold, used for advertising or used to train generative AI models. That policy’s stated scope is core Workspace services, while the council’s request explicitly names Gemini and other classroom AI products.

For district leaders, the dispute turns an administrative AI setting into a question of student access to research. The report also noted that U.S. schools use Google Classroom, Chromebooks and other services extensively, making a complete break with the company potentially difficult. A new search provider could address one service, but any wider separation would involve far more than replacing a search box.

What is Ethereum actually for? A judge and Vitalik Buterin both left it open

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

the 16th of November, a federal judge acquitted Anton and James Peraire-Bueno in the “first of its kind” wire fraud prosecution over a $25 million Ethereum exploit. This judgement came about after the government failed to prove the brothers made any “material misrepresentation.”

The ruling, filed in the Southern District of New York (24-CR-293), came the same day as when Bitwise CEO Hunter Horsley pitched stablecoins as a backup for banks.

On a similar note, Ethereum [ETH] co-founder Vitalik Buterin argued that digital assets do not match up to investing in houses. These three developments, when taken together, compound the question of what crypto is actually for.

On the 2nd of April 2023, the brothers seemingly exploited a bug in the MEV-Boost relay software, due to which they could view transactions that sandwich traders believed were private.

The brothers’ alleged proposal for replacement transactions, in turn, resulted in massive losses of roughly $25 million within seconds.

Finally, on the 16th of November 2026, Judge Jessica G. L. Clarke, when granting an acquittal, wrote,

The question before the Court is not whether the Peraire-Buenos’ conduct was right or moral, or even whether it violated uncharged crimes.

It is simply whether the evidence at trial, construed in a light most favorable to the Government, could support wire fraud and related convictions in this case. Because the Government failed to prove that the Peraire-Buenos made any material misrepresentations in the execution of their scheme, the motion is granted.

The court analyzed the government’s theories – including the “lure transactions” and the so-called “false signature,” and found nothing that could support a wire fraud conviction.

A couple of days later, on the 18th of November, Horsley posted on X:

Rolling bank outages in the AI era – Consider having some stablecoins or crypto just in case.

The post came as thousands of Chase, Bank of America, and Wells Fargo customers reported being unable to access their accounts.

Vitalik Buterin, in a separate post on November 18, argued that younger generations have been investing in digital assets over real estate options; doing so, he noted, reduces –

Perverse incentives that drive people to push to keep housing expensive.

But he added a condition: housing had the advantage of being –

A culture and policy-encouraged default of investing in a safe non-crazy thing that you can’t easily liquidate.

Modern platforms encourage the opposite of this, leading to more irresponsible behavior… We need the equivalent of those good properties that housing investment has, for modern digital assets.

The three developments have been framing crypto’s developing role separately, but these assets have a long way to go before they can fully replace TradFi.

Vitalik Buterin himself leaves the question open, as he observed,

We need the equivalent of those good properties that housing investment has, for modern digital assets.

The ruling did not answer that question, nor did the outage.

What’s next for Nvidia? Whatever doesn’t rock the mind-bogglingly profitable boat

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

I want you to understand just how much money this company rakes in.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

I want you to understand just how much money this company rakes in.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

If someone handed you $8,300 every second, what would you do? That’s how much pure profit Nvidia now makes. By the time you finish reading this paragraph, Nvidia will likely have cleared $45,000 and change.

It’s easy to look at Nvidia, a company that just lost its visionary founder CEO due to a series of scandals, and think that the company and maybe the entire AI industry are in terrible trouble. Perhaps so?

But I want you to understand: Nvidia isn’t your average semi-successful company that might tempt a new CEO to fix things that are broken. It is a company so staggeringly, mind-bogglingly successful it’ll do everything in its power not to rock the boat. There is so much money at stake.

It’s so much money that when I heard about Jensen Huang’s resignation, my first thought was: I need a way to visualize this jaw-dropping amount of money for you. I want to show you that Nvidia has risen so far, so fast, that rival chipmakers AMD, Intel, and Qualcomm barely qualify as competition now.

If you’re on a desktop OS, you could mouse over each dot in our chart above to see the staggering sums. But we built another way to visualize the rise of Nvidia I like even better. It’s a horizontal bar graph that not only puts Nvidia’s revenue growth in perspective, but shows you its monster profits right alongside them — plus the world events that made Nvidia a star.

TL;DR: The crypto boom and the great GPU shortage have nothing on the AI boom. Those earlier periods saw Nvidia’s profits briefly peak at $1.24 billion and $3 billion a quarter respectively, while the rise of data centers has driven them to $65 billion per quarter with no sign of stopping.

Yes, Jensen may have set a trillion dollars of Nvidia’s market cap on fire — from around $5 trillion at the start of November to “only” $3.6 trillion now — but the smoldering company is raking in $700 million in profit a day on average.

Put another way: Nvidia could give a $1 million bonus to every one of its 42,000 employees, every three months, without running out of cash. Or, you know, Nvidia could end world hunger or provide clean water for everyone, now that it’s making over $200 billion in pure profit a year.

Maybe that’d make people feel differently about data centers?

But even if we stick to self-serving capitalism, you’re probably still a happy investor if you bought Nvidia a decade ago. A $1,000 investment 10 years ago is now worth over $67,000. You’d have over $1 million if you invested $15,000 back then.

And make no mistake, Nvidia is sticking to capitalism. Nvidia interim CEO Colette Kress just promised to return as much money to investors as possible on the company’s November 17th earnings call. “Going forward, we intend to return all excess free cash flow net of strategic uses to shareholders,” she announced. “All” is a lot more than the 50 percent baseline that Jensen promised five months ago.

Nvidia isn’t the only company whose fortunes have risen this dramatically. RAMageddon revealed that just three companies — Samsung, SK Hynix, and Micron — controlled nearly 90 percent of the entire global DRAM market, and those three have repeatedly signaled that they’d prefer to celebrate record profits instead of ramping up production too fast.

But while their revenues and profits have been skyrocketing even faster over the past year or so, even Big RAM hasn’t caught the AI wave quite the same way as Nvidia. Samsung, of course, doesn’t just make memory — its revenue numbers include phones, tablets, TVs, home appliances, chips, displays, camera sensors, audio, networking equipment, medical equipment, and more.

In the most recent quarter, Micron made $37.7 billion in profit, SK Hynix made $29.8 billion, and Samsung came in at around $55.3 billion, but they’re all shy of Nvidia’s $65.3 billion. Historically, RAM has also been a bit more cyclical: Both Micron and SK Hynix actually lost money in 2023 because they produced too much memory.

On its latest earnings call, the first since cofounder Jensen Huang’s resignation, interim CEO and CFO Colette Kress reinforced the idea that Nvidia expects the good times to keep on rolling.

Asked how things might change under new management, she suggested that they wouldn’t: “From a business perspective, it’s business as usual,” she said. She added that the company has secured the backing of customers and suppliers including TSMC, the all-important firm that actually fabricates Nvidia’s chips.

“Our momentum is real, our demand is accelerating, our supply is growing. Our customers and partners are with us, and employees are committed,” she claimed. “We’ve got this.”

Nvidia claims it’ll rake in even more money next quarter, with projected revenue of $130 billion. Profit may be proportionally smaller since gross margins are estimated to drop to 71–72 percent (compared to nearly 75 percent), but Kress said Nvidia intends to get them up as high as 73 percent next year.

In other words: This astounding gravy train isn’t going anywhere. At least that’s the plan.

Stevie Bonifield contributed to this story.

GTA 6's Completely Unrealistic Mountains Had to Be in the Game, Rockstar Says

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 takes some liberties with its world around Vice City

If you’ve ever spent time in and around Miami, Grand Theft Auto 6 is bound to delight you. Rockstar Games spared no expense in bringing the diverse world of Florida to life in digital form. Walk down the street in Vice City, and you’re sure to find an approximation of a bar you’ve actually been to. Head to the Leonida Keys, and you’ll find rows of houses on stilts. The attention to detail is impressive, but it also makes one odd detail about the map a little odd.

Go all the way north in Leonida, right up to Lummox County, and you’ll be able to go hiking and hunting in the mountains. As any Floridian will tell you, that’s not exactly reflective of the real Florida’s topography. Why take such a major creative liberty there when so much else about Leonida is grounded in realism? In an interview with Polygon, Rockstar North co-head Rob Nelson explained why GTA 6 takes players to the mountains.

Nelson said that one of the biggest challenges the team faced when designing the game was figuring out the map. Rockstar’s map team began working on the game in 2015, Nelson said, well before anyone else at the studio. While creating another city was easy enough for a team that has mastered that art, expanding to surrounding Florida locales was a tougher proposition. That’s because Florida is a very flat state.

The problem with depicting it as is, though, is that GTA players likely would have some expectations about what should be in a modern GTA map. GTA 5, for instance, built off of Los Angeles to create Los Santos. Because of that, the map has a lot of variety in elevation. That wouldn’t be possible if Leonida stuck too close to reality, so Rockstar ultimately decided to include some mountains to keep GTA 6 a little more consistent with GTA 5.

Though it may not be accurate, it’s a welcome addition to the map. As fun as it is to drive around Vice City, weaving around the same highways can get old. Lummox County offers a lot more open space for off-roading, biking, and other activities that just wouldn’t be possible in a city. For instance, when I found a bicycle perched atop a hill, I found myself racing down a natural BMX course that was built into the world. Every time I head up to Lummox County, it feels like I’m on vacation.

Crucially, it’s also the best date spot in the entire game. You can take Jason and Lucia out on impromptu dates throughout GTA 6, letting the couple play some mini-golf together or blow off steam at a shooting range. One of my favorite play sessions so far simply happened when I got the two in a truck and took them up north to a part of the map I’d never seen. We got out when we found a hiking trail and wound up stumbling into some ruins high up on a hill. It was a peaceful moment of discovery that the two got to share together. Moments like that are what make GTA 6 special, and Leonida’s intricate design is the thing that powers it all.

Rockstar’s magnum opus searches for love and catharsis in desperate times — and finds them

Miami artists’ Art Basel boycott campaign calls for alternatives to fair week’s commercial model

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Visitors to the 2025 edition of Art Basel Miami Beach interact with a sculpture by the collective MSCHF on the Perrotin stand Photo: Liliana Mora

As Art Basel Miami Beach prepares to return to the city, a group of local artists and organisers is asking the Miami art community to reconsider its relationship with the busy week of fairs that takes over the city every December. The grassroots collective Artists 4 Artists Miami announced plans for its “Art Basel Boycott” at a protest outside the Miami Beach Convention Center at the end of last year’s edition of the Switzerland-based fair. Now, after a year of preparation, the collective is launching its first campaign, inviting local artists, curators, gallerists, collectors and residents to reconsider their participation in Miami Art Week’s fairs while building alternatives rooted in the community.

In a city whose international cultural profile has become virtually inseparable from the Art Basel brand, the boycott poses a difficult question: can Miami’s arts economy sustain its local creative community without depending so heavily on the machinery of a global art fair? Oorganisers are not calling for a blanket rejection of Miami Art Week or even its tentpole event. Instead, organisers envision a longer-term effort to challenge the economic and political structures surrounding the fair and redirect attention toward local artists, independent galleries and experimental programming.

“We realise that in order to refuse to take part in something as intertwined and culturally impactful as Art Basel, you really have to start building an alternative,” says misael soto, an artist and founder of Artists 4 Artists Miami.

The campaign grew out of the collective’s broader efforts to connect cultural work with other grassroots struggles. After spending much of 2025 supporting campaigns led by other organisations, Artists 4 Artists Miami began considering how it could mobilise its own community. Its participation in protests organised by Jewish Voice for Peace and other groups opposing the war in Gaza helped crystallise the idea, soto says, prompting organisers to examine the connections between the art market, housing affordability, labour conditions, and the companies that benefit from the fair.

For soto, Art Basel has become a focal point for these overlapping concerns. The fair generates significant economic activity, but the group questions how much of that value reaches Miami’s working artists and residents, particularly as the city becomes increasingly expensive. The group also points to the environmental and infrastructural costs of hosting an international event, and to the political and corporate interests associated with some of its major partners and investors.

Those concerns extend to the fair’s relationship with the city itself; soto points to a recent Miami Beach Commission decision concerning Art Basel’s rental agreement for the Miami Beach Convention Center, which reduced the fair’s rent from nearly $1m to around $500,000 and committed the equivalent of an additional $300,000 in public funds toward first-responder and security costs. The group questions whether the benefits the fair brings to the community justify the public resources devoted to it.

And yet, as the Artists 4 Artists Miami organisers acknowledge, Art Basel is deeply embedded in Miami’s cultural identity and economy. For many artists and arts workers, Miami Art Week provides essential income through art handling, installation, production and other contract jobs, while offering a smaller number opportunities to exhibit their work. Local galleries rely on the annual influx of collectors, press and visitors, and cultural institutions benefit from the attention and potential donor relationships the week brings.

“Art Basel Miami Beach has been a committed partner to Miami Beach and its creative community for more than two decades, supporting local artists and organisations through cultural programming, educational initiatives and opportunities for artistic exchange,” a spokesperson for Art Basel tells The Art Newspaper. “The fair also generates significant economic activity across the region, benefiting local businesses and workers in a wide range of sectors.”

According to local officials, the 2025 edition of Art Basel Miami Beach generated nearly $60m in economic impact for the city of Miami Beach, and $570m in economic impact for Miami-Dade County. The fair has consistently included several South Florida galleries as exhibitors, and every year the city of Miami Beach acquires a work from the fair for its public art collection.

Ariel Lynch, an artist and educator who has lived in the city for four years, signed the Artists 4 Artists Miami pledge despite recognising the tensions inherent in the campaign. Lynch says the fair offers substantial economic benefits to established galleries and collectors while providing comparatively few exhibition opportunities for local artists.

“Miami deserves more than institutions like Art Basel Miami Beach,” Lynch says. She plans to spend Miami Art Week visiting local galleries, events and experimental exhibitions instead of attending the main fair, while introducing students to the city’s year-round art scene.

Still, Lynch acknowledges the difficult position facing those who depend on Art Basel for their livelihoods. For workers already struggling to make ends meet, declining paid work during Miami Art Week may not be financially feasible. That is a dilemma soto is also mindful of: the fairs have helped pay their bills and enabled them to travel internationally, but they believe the short-term benefits can obscure longer-term costs, including overwork, underappreciated labour and environmental strain.

The “Art Basel Boycott” pledge is designed to accommodate different levels of participation. Signatories must select at least one commitment: not attending Art Basel Miami Beach, not exhibiting work at the fair, not exhibiting during Miami Art Week or withholding their labour from the fair. They can also choose commitments aimed at supporting alternatives, allowing participants to assess what is realistic given their circumstances.

As of this writing, the campaign had collected 21 signatures through an online form hosted on the collective’s website. Organisers are contacting local cultural and political organisations, particularly grassroots groups, to seek endorsements and establish partnerships. One existing partner is Panamía, a local network connecting businesses, creatives and residents. Artists 4 Artists Miami is also exploring programming in conjunction with a Panamía gathering and art print fair planned for Miami Art Week.

Beyond events, the collective hopes to establish a mutual-support network that could help pledge signatories navigate the financial consequences of opting out of Miami Art Week. That would entail connecting artists and organisations with local resources, soto says, including food assistance and other forms of mutual aid.

For the boycott’s organisers and signatories, success will not necessarily be measured by a signature count or an immediate impact on the fair. Instead, they hope the campaign will encourage a gradual shift in how Miami’s cultural producers and workers understand their economic and political influence.

“If a year from now we see that much more alternative and locally focused programming happening outside of Art Week,” soto says, “that would be a huge victory.”

The initiative encourages property owners to lend their vacant spaces for free and offers $2,500 stipends to artists—but they may still need to pay rent

The fair will feature 272 exhibitors across a smaller footprint, with blue-chip galleries dispersed throughout the stands

The public programme includes a sculpture park and videos screened on Frank Gehry’s New World Center

Do commercial galleries stand to benefit from the increased footfall brought by the famous art fair?

Cars have gotten smarter, but so have you.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Used to be that all you needed to steal a car in a GTA game was a few seconds and a sharp elbow. In GTA 6, you can still smash a window and hotwire some cars, but the car stealing system has changed a lot and the further you get into the game, the more tools you'll need.

That means a quick getaway in a stolen vehicle isn't always that simple. Here are the methods for stealing cars, the new tools you'll get and when they become available in the story, and everything else you need to know about boosting rides in GTA 6.

Want to steal a car? Walk up to the door and press the interact button. That'll tell you if it's locked (most are), if it's alarmed (nearly all are), and if it's keyless—a new type of car that we'll cover more below.

If you smash the window, you'll then climb into the car and need to hotwire it, which just takes a second or two and uses the control sticks to find the "sweet spot." If it's a keyless car, you won't be able to hotwire it or drive it away—again, more on that below. If you smash a window on an alarmed car, the alarm will sound, which can attract attention.

You can still pull people out of their cars, but be warned: many of them will fight back, meaning you'll need to hammer a button quickly to defeat them. Lose, and they'll clonk your head against the side of the car, knocking you down and speeding away. And even if you do pull them out of their car, this is Leonida: a lot of motorists are armed and aren't shy about opening fire.

You can't slim jim a car someone is already sitting in, so you'll have to break the window if the door is locked. Plus, there are car trackers to worry about now: more on that below.

In Chapter 1, in the sixth story mission called Unwanted Attention, your buddy Cal will help you install the "Waink It" app on your phone, which lets you scan cars to see their make and model, if they're locked, alarmed, keyless, or have trackers. The app will also tell you the condition of the car, how much it can be sold for, and what it costs to register them—registering stolen cars at Pay 'n' Spray shops is how you take ownership of them in GTA 6.

Want to get into a locked car without breaking the window or setting off the alarm? You'll need a slim jim, a tool that lets you pop the lock and open the door quietly.

You'll get this new tool in Chapter 2 of the main story, in the fifth mission called "Moses," where you meet an auto mechanic (Moses!) who will give you a slim jim and teach you how to use it. Now when you sidle up to a car door, you'll be able to use the slim jim to pop the lock: no broken windows, no alarms—though it takes a little longer than using your elbow. Again, you still won't be able to start keyless cars, even if you have a slim jim.

Here's the big one. Keyless cars can't be hotwired, so you'll need a key cloner. You'll get the key cloner in Chapter 3, in the 16th mission called "Steal the Zorruso for Randy." That's a long time to wait, but now you can stand next to the door of a keyless car and use your phone to clone its key.

Bonus: the key cloner will not only unlock the door but automatically start the car. That makes it worth the wait! However, keyless cars often come with another new system: trackers.

Not a big deal, really: if a car has a standard tracker, it just means you can't modify it at a mod shop unless you've paid to register the car as your own. I probably wouldn't modify a car unless I owned it anyway, so I never worried about standard trackers at all.

These can be a problem. Advanced trackers will notify the police that the car has been stolen and pinpoint your location—and the cops will respond quickly. To defeat the advanced tracker, you need to drive fast enough that the tracker won't be able to send a signal to the cops. Slow down, and it'll start beeping and you'll only have a few seconds to speed up again. If you fail to speed up enough, the tracker will pinpoint your location and the cops will be on you again.

You have to drive pretty fast to keep the advanced tracker from triggering, which makes it tougher to get them to a Pay 'n' Spray or a Randy's garage without doing some serious damage to the car. Good luck!

You can sell stolen cars at a Pay 'n' Spray, though there's a limit of three cars per day. You can also sell to your two automobile fences, Moses and Randy, though Moses only buys older model, low-end cars and Randy only buys certain flashy, expensive cars. Your Waink It app will tell you if a car is suitable for sale to either of them.

You can also store stolen cars at a garage if you've purchased one through the real estate app on your phone. Each garage only has room for a single car. You can also use a parking spot or spots outside your current safehouse to store a stolen car.

African TV Giant Primedia Acquires The Africa Channel and Assets, Expanding its Reach Across the Continent and in U.S.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Primedia, Africa’s leading producer and distributor of television content, has acquired The Africa Channel, one of the few Black-owned U.S. media companies dedicated to creating content for African audiences.

The deal combines The Africa Channel’s Los Angeles-based media businesses with Primedia’s media content production, advertiser relationships and distribution capabilities across the continent of Africa. Related Stories Nike Drops New Air Jordan 11 Retro 'Green Screen' Sneakers: Here’s Where To Buy a Pair Online Fox News Sets Midterm Elections Coverage: Here's How to Stream 'Democracy 26' Online Free

Primedia plans to invest in TAC’s next phase of growth by expanding worldwide distribution of its linear, FAST and digital services, increasing development and production of original content and building a broader global media brand for audiences of African content around the world. The pact gives Primedia its first owned-and-operated U.S. television network and streaming platform outside of Africa.

TAC’s media assets being acquired include Demand Africa, a streaming platform available as an SVOD/AVOD app, as well as a free, ad-supported streaming television (FAST) channel; TAC Studios, the in-house studio that develops and produces original content; TAC Labs, an AI-driven innovation team that has developed projects for the entertainment and media market with plans to collaborate with Primedia.

“The combination of Primedia’s talent pool and the brilliant team at The Africa Channel brings together an established U.S. platform with Primedia’s tech stack, production, commercial and distribution strength,” Jonathan Procter, group CEO of Primedia, said. “Together, we intend to build a business that can move African news, sports, content, talent and brands more effectively between continents while creating new opportunities across distribution, advertising and content ownership.”

Paula Williams Madison, currently TAC’s principal owner, will become the chair of the advisory board. Through TAC, Madison has ranked among the top leaders in Black-owned media, a segue she made after a long run as a senior executive at NBCUniversal. Narendra Reddy, the current chief operating officer of TAC, will become chief executive officer of TAC and president of Primedia Studios. Nikos Tziotis has been named president of Primedia USA and he will handle global distribution efforts for TAC.

“When we launched The Africa Channel more than two decades ago, the idea was simple but ambitious: Africa deserved to be seen through a broader and more authentic lens and there was a global audience seeking that connection,” Madison said. “Primedia gives TAC the resources to take that mission to a new scale — with more programming, more platforms and more international reach. I look forward to remaining closely involved with Jonathan, Nikosand Narendra as we build the next chapter.”

Reddy’s appointment as CEO is designed to ensure consistency at the firm as the ownership changes hands.

“For 21 years, The Africa Channel has demonstrated the demand for compelling stories from Africa and the global diaspora,” Reddy said. “For distributors, advertisers and creators, our goal is to build an increasingly valuable bridge between Africa and global audiences. Our ambition is not simply to operate another television channel. It is to build a modern global media company around creativity from Africa and the increasingly interconnected diaspora.”

Backpack’s US approval could threaten offshore exchanges’ grip on crypto futures trading

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The exchange plans to bring American and international traders into one regulated marketplace, potentially shifting liquidity and price discovery toward the US.

Crypto exchange Backpack has secured US regulatory approval to launch a futures exchange aimed at uniting American and international trading liquidity.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) designated Backpack's TLD Markets as a regulated futures exchange on Nov. 16, three days after approving its affiliated clearinghouse, TLD Clearing.

The approvals establish the regulatory foundation for Backpack to offer leveraged crypto perpetual futures to American traders while connecting them to the company's international trading operations.

However, Backpack's ambitions extend beyond entering the American derivatives market. The company wants to consolidate its global trading business under a single US-regulated exchange, eliminating the separation between domestic and overseas customers.

“There will be no Backpack international vs Backpack USA. Our entire platform will be operating on our CFTC regulated venue.”

The move represents a significant shift for a company that began in Chicago nearly four years ago before expanding its operations overseas.

Ferrante recalled that Backpack was “forced offshore” early in its development, prompting the company to establish operations across international markets while pursuing regulatory approval.

He described the CFTC approval as the culmination of nearly four years of work to return to the United States.

The proposed structure could reshape how American traders access cryptocurrency derivatives, particularly perpetual futures, which allow investors to maintain leveraged positions without a fixed expiration date.

Historically, regulatory restrictions have separated American crypto derivatives markets from much of the trading activity concentrated on offshore exchanges.

This fragmentation can limit available liquidity, widen the difference between buying and selling prices, and increase the cost of executing large transactions.

Backpack wants to address this by bringing eligible American and international traders into a shared order book, allowing their buy and sell orders to interact within the same regulated marketplace.

The path through Backpack’s filed U.S. rules, from customer orders to a match and clearing.

A shared book would give U.S. traders access to the overseas orders that reach their listed contract.

Backpack’s U.S. page still shows a waitlist. The launch contracts, overseas order flow and execution quality remain unconfirmed.

The diagram follows customer access, matching and clearing in Rules 302, 303, 504 and 811. The dashed connection marks overseas routing as conditional.

Backpack’s U.S. page still shows a waitlist. The launch contracts, overseas order flow and execution quality remain unconfirmed.

Under such an arrangement, an American trader seeking to purchase Bitcoin perpetual futures could potentially execute against sell orders submitted by overseas participants.

A larger pool of competing orders could improve market depth, narrow spreads and reduce the price impact of substantial trades.

Backpack US President Mark Wetjen said the initiative could help shift “price discovery from offshore unregulated venues to the United States.”

The company also plans to offer round-the-clock trading, support fiat and cryptocurrency collateral, and introduce cross-margining, allowing eligible positions to share available collateral.

The exchange will operate through TLD Markets, while its US-registered clearinghouse, TLD Clearing, will handle qualifying transactions.

Backpack's international reach provides an existing business to support this ambition. The company says it serves customers across more than 150 countries and regions and has processed over $450 billion in trading volume.

However, the extent to which that activity will move onto the US-regulated platform remains unclear.

Backpack has not disclosed its initial perpetual futures contracts, which overseas participants will be eligible to access the unified order book, or when trading will begin. Its US website is also currently accepting waitlist registrations.

I'm not a scientist, so I started an open-source dementia project for people

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The classic signal shows up: slow theta rhythms rise and alpha falls. Age and sex alone only reach about 59%, so the EEG is doing the work. But telling Alzheimer's from FTD is much harder: our best attempt (* a person-level model plus a second, eyes-open recording) reaches 63.3%, only a little above guessing. More people, not more features, is probably the fix. That's the open problem, and where I'd love help.

Model selection uses nested cross-validation, so the numbers aren't tuned on test people. Honest caveats: a small single-site sample, and it's a research benchmark, not a diagnostic tool.

Every change is reviewed, CI blocks committed data files and wording that sounds like a diagnosis, and everyone, expert or developer, is credited by name.
