Where To Play Every Game Of The Year Nominee For 2026

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The votes have been cast and the nominees for 2026's Game of the Year have been revealed. Six of the past year's biggest and best games, and as always, the games that have and haven't made the cut are being hotly debated. There's also a remake in the mix this year along with a game that hadn't been released when the nominations were announced, so a lot for those who think the awards should have clearer criteria to discuss.

Whether you agree with all six nominees or not, it's hard to argue that the games in the running this year aren't six of the six biggest games released this calendar year. The games that, if you haven't played anything else this year, or if there are some you missed, you should probably try your best to play before we get to 2027 and another year of new games begins. Whether you're wondering whether you have the right hardware before buying the nominees, want to know if they have a physical release, or you just want to know how much they cost, you'll find everything you need to know to fill in your GOTY gaps below.

GTA 6 is finally here, and its Game of the Year nomination has caused a stir. The game will be here by the time the awards are handed out, but it was nominated two days before release. That doesn't matter to you right now, though. The launch of GTA 6 is mere hours away as I type this, and if you're not one of the 15 million people who have placed a pre-order, here's what you need to know.

As long as you ignore the state of rent, wages, and world economics, GTA 6's price tag isn't that bad, you guys!

GTA 6 will only be available on PS5 and Xbox Series X|S when released. Its PC launch will follow at a later, still unrevealed, time. There are two editions to choose from - standard and ultimate. The standard edition will provide tens, if not hundreds, of hours of entertainment, but there will be various stores, barbers, and tattoo parlors that are off-limits if you don't own the ultimate edition.

The standard edition of GTA 6 is $79.99 on both PS5 and Series X|S, while the ultimate edition is $99.99 on both platforms.

GTA 6 isn't the only game that has caused a stir by picking up a GOTY nomination this year. The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time has poked the beehive that is remakes being eligible to win the prestigious award. If there was ever a remake that was going to qualify, it's a universally loved one like this that somehow reimagines one of the greatest games of all time without upsetting any of the original's fans.

There is only one place you can play the Ocarina of Time remake, and that's on Switch 2. There are no different editions to choose from, but there is a physical release, and since this is a Switch 2 exclusive, there's a price difference between its digital and physical versions. A digital copy of Ocarina of Time costs $59.99, but if you want a physical copy, it'll cost you $69.99.

Robert Pattinson might be the next James Bond on the big screen, but Patrick Gibson became the video game Bond this year in the critically acclaimed 007 First Light. A performance so good that not only were fans suggesting Gibson could potentially make the jump to Bond movies, but the odds of that happening spiked significantly after the game was released.

First Light is the first nominee on the list that's available to play on Steam. It's also on PS5 and Series X|S, and while a Switch 2 version was promised, it has been delayed until March 18, 2027. I have played a little bit of the game on Switch 2, and for what it's worth, I didn't encounter any issues and was a little surprised that its release was moved into 2027.

It's amazing to see just how far this series has come since the low point that was Resident Evil 6. The trilogy that has followed, alongside the remakes of games from its heyday, has put Resident Evil firmly back on top of the video game mountain. That's why Resident Evil Requiem, a game many would describe as the best new Resi game in decades, is in the running to be crowned Game of the Year.

If you're trying to play all of these games before a winner is crowned, or you want to get one under your belt quickly, Requiem is the place to start. By far the shortest nominee on this year's GOTY list, with a story that takes around 11 hours to play through according to How Long To Beat. It's also the only one of all six nominees that's available on all major platforms, with those who played on Switch 2 awarding it, and Capcom's other day-one Switch 2 releases this year, high praise.

Phantom Blade Zero is a game people have been waiting a long time for. Shown off for the first time well over three years ago, S-Game's action-packed epic finally arrived in October, and it did not disappoint. Rave reviews and a player base that wholeheartedly agrees with what the critics had to say about it have landed it among 2026's Game of the Year nominees.

Your platform options are limited for this one - just PS5 for console players, but it is on PC too if you prefer to play there. There's no physical release either, which is unfortunate, although a physical edition of the PS5 version has been promised for 2027. Hopefully there aren't any issues getting that out on time, with PlayStation pushing forward with its promise to stop putting games on discs in 2028.

Control Resonant might be the most surprising inclusion among this year's Game of the Year nominees. Not because it doesn't deserve to be on this list, but because it's the only game of the six where the combined reaction to its release wasn't overwhelmingly positive, unlike the other five. Not that many people had anything negative to say about it, just that it didn't seem to blow everyone away quite as much as its GOTY competitors.

Now that said, if you want to play the best games 2026 has to offer, Control Resonant should unquestionably be on your list, particularly if you liked the first game and you're an Alan Wake fan. It's available on PS5, Xbox, and PC, there's a physical version if you're into that kind of thing, and it's slightly more expensive and includes an extra outfit, resources, and a digital artbook and soundtrack.

Steam has incredible sales all year round, but nothing quite compares to the deals you can get during the holiday season!

The studio pulled the video after a wave of complaints and said replacing the casters who actually worked the tournament with AI voices "wasn’t the right call."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

XFollowing a backlash from casters and esports fans, Embark Studios has taken down a recap video from a recent esports tournament for The Finals that replaced the original casters' voices with AI.

The commentators for the APAC Closed Qualifiers for The Grand Major, the annual championship tournament for Embark's team-based FPS, were human. But, as reported by IGN, a highlight video released by Embark built from clips from the qualifier replaced those casters with audio from the game's AI commentators, Scotty and June.

It did not go over well. "After 4 cycles, open/closed quals, the time, effort, passion and emotion myself @TheeHyperactive and @bluepanda_casts put in," The Finals caster ProtoANZ wrote on X. "We get replaced by two AI casters who even quote us :/ I get they are apart of the game but yikes. Thanks @reachthefinals."

Reposting ProtoANZ's message, Bluepanda added, "For nearly 7 years I've been repeatedly told that my work is valued and appreciated only to then have the same people saying those things turn around and either completely ghost me or tell me they're going in a different direction with talent and that my services aren't needed GG."

XOther casters soon weighed in to express support for ProtoANZ and Bluepanda: League of Legends caster Sam "Initialise" Hapgood called it "dystopian," while Jen "LemonKiwi" Pichette described it as "appalling" and said "REAL casters deserve better."

Embark's relationship with AI in game development has always been messy. It recently announced a new "dynamic commentary" feature for The Finals that enables Scotty and June, the game's AI commentators, to call out specific gameplay events and individual player names, and earlier this month it defended the use of AI voices in Arc Raiders, saying it was "too impractical to record every possible version" of in-game items because the voice actors—people who literally make their living reading scripts—"would just go crazy if they had to read all of that."

That has resulted in some dissenting voices in the mix, and a few responses to ProtoANZ's message opined that this was merely a natural and predictable evolution of Embark's approach.

Keep up to date with the most important stories and the best deals, as picked by the PC Gamer team.

In a message posted to X, however, Embark community and competitive operations lead Oscar Lundberg apologized for replacing the casters' voices with AI, saying it was a "mistake."

"We intended for Scotty and June to help bring more casual interest into The Finals esports ahead of TGM. That was the goal, but the work of our talent deserved to be included and highlighted, and it wasn’t," Lundberg wrote. "The video is down and won’t return in this form. The AMER and EMEA recaps planned in the same format won’t go out as planned either, and we’re rethinking all three."

Embark expressed similar sentiments in a statement shared with IGN, saying that replacing caster voices with AI "wasn't the right call" and expressing gratitude for the "candid feedback."

But that didn't fly with the casters at the heart of the matter. "Embark saw no issue with this until they received overwhelming global backlash and even then their response was essentially, 'Whoopsie, we didn't think people would mind'," Bluepanda told the site. "It says to me that they're not really sorry at all. I'm done with the game and esports as a whole at this stage, the effort we put in and the amount of bullshit we have to deal with, especially in OCE, just isn't worth it anymore."

Calling the situation "a shit show," ProtoANZ called for the creation of an esports talent union to protect commentary and production teams: "We'll turn around and say, 'We're not doing this until people send us contracts that essentially ban the use of our voices in generative AI'."

These MacBook Deals Are Good Enough to Shop Before Black Friday

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

Considering the price of tech, we’ll gladly take $250 off a new MacBook.

If there’s one Apple product line where the recent price hikes hit the hardest, it’s the MacBook. Rising RAM and storage costs have pushed laptop prices up across the board, which makes scoring a new MacBook on sale all the more mission critical.

As luck would have it, Black Friday deals on Apple tech are already ramping up on Amazon, including discounts on MacBooks. While there’s always the chance these deals might continue to deepen in the next week, the current discounts are nothing to sneeze at if you can’t wait that long to shop.

Of course, not all MacBooks were created equal. While the standard MacBook Air is great for most, those who are planning on running hardcore software might be benefit from a beefed-up MacBook Pro. And if budget is your primary concern, we’re of the opinion that the MacBook Neo is a worthy buy, even if it’s only $50 off right now.

The best MacBook Air deal you’ll find right now is this $250 discount on the 15-inch model. With this laptop, you’ll get Apple’s M5 chip in a fanless, 3.3-pound frame that handles heavy multitasking and light video editing without breaking a sweat. Base storage doubled to 512 GB this year—a great upgrade for storing more apps, photos, and files—and the 15.3-inch Liquid Retina display is sharp and vivid. You also get a 12 MP Center Stage camera that’s great on video calls, WiFi 7 for fast downloads, and up to 18 hours of battery, enough for a busy workday and then some. It won’t replace a MacBook Pro for serious creative work or gaming, but for school, business, and general use, it’s the one to get.

Deals on 13-inch models aren’t as steep—yet. But we expect that to change in a week’s time.

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 15-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 512 GB)

Apple 2026 MacBook Air 13-Inch (M5, 16 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Given that Apple just dropped a new slate of M6 MacBook Pros, in addition to the company’s first-ever touchscreen laptop, we were anticipating discounts on the M5 range to clear out inventory. In the lead-up to Black Friday, you can shave $200 off of the 14-inch model, complete with a Liquid Retina XDR display with 120Hz ProMotion, up to 24 hours of battery, three Thunderbolt 4 ports, HDMI, an SD card slot, and up to 2 TB of storage.

Why go Pro over the Air? The GPU is fast, which makes a difference when editing video, working with high-res photos, or gaming, and the XDR screen is significantly brighter. If your daily workload has started becoming too much for the Air to handle, this is where you should look next. If you want the biggest, most powerful model possible, souped-up MacBook Pros are up to $500 off.

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 16-Inch (M5 Max, 36 GB RAM, 2 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 14-Inch (M5 Pro, 24 GB RAM, 1 TB)

Apple 2025 MacBook Pro 16-Inch (M5 Pro, 24 GB RAM, 2 TB)

The MacBook Neo is Apple’s most affordable laptop, even with the recent price hike from $599 to $699. Right now, the Neo is modestly discounted, although we’re holding out hope that it might drop down to its inaugural price—or even lower—once Black Friday proper arrives. The Neo is an easy recommendation for anyone looking for their first Mac. It runs on Apple’s A18 Pro chip and is surprisingly quick for browsing, sending email, and streaming video. The 13-inch Liquid Retina display looks great for the price, and the battery lasts a solid 16 hours. It also comes in four fun colors, with the yellow-green citrus being a fan favorite.

There are some trade-offs with going with Apple’s budget model, including storage that starts at 256 GB, just 8 GB of RAM, and no keyboard backlight. You also get only two USB-C ports, so getting a USB-C hub is a must if you plan to connect a lot of accessories. Spending an extra $100 gets you a Neo with 512 GB of storage and a Touch ID sensor for quick unlocking. For students or anyone who mostly sends emails and browses the Web, the Neo is hard to beat.

Fed minutes: almost all officials expect further rate hikes will likely be appropriate

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Two Fed members voted against holding rates at 3.75% to 4% in October, preferring a quarter-point increase

Renovation work continues on the Marriner S. Eccles Federal Reserve Board Building, the main offices of the Board of Governors of the Federal Reserve System on December 9, 2025 in Washington, DC. (Andrew Harnik/Getty Images)

Federal Reserve officials concluded in October that additional rate hikes would be warranted to bring inflation back to 2%, according to minutes the central bank released Wednesday.

Nearly all participants at the Oct. 27-28 meeting concluded that, based on their baseline forecasts for prices and employment, lifting the federal funds target range "would likely be appropriate." A couple of participants said an increase of more than 25 basis points could warrant consideration at the next meeting if incoming inflation data were unfavorable.

The Federal Open Market Committee voted 10-2 to hold the benchmark rate at 3.75% to 4%. Fed Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack dissented, preferring a quarter-point increase. The next meeting is Dec. 8-9.

Many participants viewed the AI buildout as "a material source of inflation risk." Some said the investment surge could raise aggregate demand relative to supply if capacity expansion proved slower than expected. Many said demand for AI-related products was straining supply and raising prices of technology-related consumer goods.

The minutes also show concern about AI financing. Fed staff highlighted rapid growth in AI-related debt and said that leasing obligations kept off balance sheets, along with circular funding deals among AI-linked companies, could magnify losses if AI investments failed to deliver the expected returns.

Most participants noted a rapid rise in AI-related borrowing, much of it arranged through intricate, hard-to-see financing structures. A few said risk tied to AI was increasingly clustered in a handful of companies. Almost all participants commented on the risks of a scenario in which AI returns disappointed investors.

Staff estimated that total inflation, measured by the 12-month change in the personal consumption expenditures price index, rose to 3.6% in September. Core inflation edged up to 3.1%.

Fed officials have voiced differing views on how fast to raise interest rates. Fed Governor Christopher Waller told CNBC on Wednesday that the Fed should consider a half-point increase in December and may have to "shock" people.

New York Fed President John Williams said Tuesday, “I don’t think the situation calls for dramatic action,” according to CNBC. St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem said Tuesday the economy “will likely require several interest rate increases before the spring of 2027.”

Delete GTA 6 from your hard drive and indie publisher Devolver Digital might give you a free game

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GTA 6 is out at midnight everywhere (19th November), but it's far from the only game in town. Indie publisher Devolver Digital is taking on the open-world behemoth with its very own Volvy's Adventure: Reslimed, out the same day, and you can win a free copy of the platformer by proving you've deleted GTA 6 off your hard drive.

"Want a free copy of November's hottest release? Repost this tweet with a video of you deleting your GTA6 pre-load along with #MakeRoomForVolvy for a chance to win a copy of Volvy's Adventure: Reslimed on Steam," the publisher wrote on social media.

It's not every day you see a publisher promote its own games by asking folks to delete another company's release, but arguably the wildest part of the giveaway is that Volvy's Adventure isn't launching on consoles. Even if you were to scrub GTA 6's 212GB pre-load from your PS5 or Xbox, you're definitely not making room for Volvy.

"Heads up: Volvy's Adventure: Reslimed is launching exclusively on PC. While we expect to see console GTA deletions in your submissions, Steam keys are the only prize available for this giveaway," Devolver noted.

Devolver made quite an amusing GTA 6 parody trailer, as well, which sees Volvy and Miss Volvy running from the police and getting steamy in the shower. I've embedded it up above for your viewing pleasure.

For the unaware, Volvy used to be a common fixture at Devolver Digital's frequently bonkers notE3 presentations. He's now somewhat of a company mascot. But while Volvy's Adventure: Reslimed is being presented as a "much-anticipated remaster of the original remake of the hit action-platformer," this is, in fact, Volvy's first proper game. Making up an elaborate history for Volvy is just part of the publisher's long-running and increasingly questionable gag.

Gunbrella and Gato Robot developer doinksoft is behind this one, a throwback side-scrolling platformer where our mouse-like mascot uses his oversized hand to helicopter over gaps, punch through roadblocks and, erm, fist himself underground, per the game's Steam page.

Devolver Digital has been waiting to steal Rockstar's thunder for well over a year now. The publisher threatened to release a game on GTA 6's release date way back in May 2025, and has pushed back its plan with each new GTA 6 delay. Gotta respect the commitment, at least.

GTA 6 won't let you use cheats until you complete the main story, and locks progression when you do

Tech lawsuits defined 2026 — and lawmakers want more

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Congress is scrambling to provide legal recourse for deepfakes and online grooming.

Congress is scrambling to provide legal recourse for deepfakes and online grooming.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

With just a few weeks remaining, lawmakers are trying to pass new protections for victims of online harm before the end of 2026, targeting deepfake creators and online platforms that block users from filing lawsuits.

On Tuesday, members of a Senate Judiciary subcommittee hosted a hearing calling for the end of forced arbitration for online harms. Their immediate target was Roblox, which they accused of evading legal complaints about children allegedly groomed on the platform by enforcing its own forced arbitration clause against users who agreed to the terms as minors, in some cases even under the age of 10. Separately, a few blocks away, Reps. Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez (D-NY) and Laurel Lee (R-FL) brought hotel heiress Paris Hilton to Capitol Hill to advocate for the Defiance Act, which would let victims of nonconsensual intimate deepfakes sue their creators.

“The best thing we can do in terms of enforcement is we can open up the courthouse doors,” Sen. Josh Hawley (R-MO), who chairs the Judiciary subcommittee, told reporters after the Roblox hearing. “The more I see these companies evade regulations, the more I think there is nothing as powerful as a jury.” Hawley introduced a new bill with ranking member Dick Durbin (D-IL) to render arbitration agreements unenforceable for disputes over alleged child sexual exploitation or “child cyber injury,” which could include “reputational harm, mental suffering,” or death.

“The best thing we can do in terms of enforcement is we can open up the courthouse doors”

Roblox has sought to enforce its arbitration clause in lawsuits it’s facing over allegations that users were groomed on the gaming platform as minors, despite the company’s claims of protections. Roblox is facing about 180 lawsuits in a consolidated case over its alleged role in failing to protect children. Under the arbitration process, the public would not get to see legal discovery like the kind that’s come to light under recent cases against Meta. Hawley called the jury system “corruption-proof” and “an incredibly big hammer.”

Roblox has denied that it’s trying to dodge accountability. “Safety has been central to Roblox since we launched. We require age checks for chat, place users under 16 in age-based accounts with protective defaults, and block images and video in chat,” Roblox’s global head of public policy Nicky Jackson Colaço said in a statement. “A legal position about arbitration is not a measure of a company’s safety record, and a hearing built around it fails to advance safety.”

Congress has only a little more than three more weeks left in session before the end of the year, and a new Democratic majority will be sworn in come January. Republicans are expected to do their best to push through President Donald Trump’s nominees for judgeships and executive branch posts like FCC commissioner in the lame-duck session, as well as try to pass year-end bills like the National Defense Authorization Act, which often includes a range of other policy items. But some lawmakers are holding out hope that they can open up courthouses to new litigation over deepfake abuse and child predation.

Litigation has become one of the more successful ways for survivors of online harm to seek change. Congress has failed to pass many prominent child safety, privacy, and consumer protection bills, though it has begun to tackle the deepfake issue through the Take It Down Act. At the same time, attorneys general and individual plaintiffs have collected significant settlements and legal wins despite the broad scope of tech’s legal shield, Section 230. If bills like the Defiance Act or those seeking to end forced arbitration were to pass, tech companies and perpetrators of online harms could become vulnerable to a new wave of legal challenges.

“You will be amazed by how quickly the company will change its behavior once it is sued in public court”

In addition to Hawley and Durbin’s bill about forced arbitration, Sens. Richard Blumenthal (D-CT), Katie Britt (R-AL), and Cory Booker (D-NJ) introduced a similar bill with a slightly different approach, including explicitly covering data privacy violations. At the Roblox hearing, Northwestern University law professor Myriam Gilles, an expert on forced arbitration, said she’s testified before Congress many times on this topic, but “never expected to come before this body to talk about its use against 9 year olds.” Passing either proposal, she said, would have a profound impact. “You will be amazed by how quickly the company will change its behavior once it is sued in public court.”

The Defiance Act would let victims of nonconsensual intimate deepfakes sue their creators for damages, in an attempt to raise the cost of creating these kinds of images. Hilton visited Washington earlier this year to share how an intimate non-deepfake video of her at age 19 shared without her consent impacted her life, and she used her story to advocate for the Defiance Act. At Tuesday’s press conference, she said she would come back to Congress as many times as necessary for the bill to become law. “Some people might have thought that was the end of my fight,” Hilton said. “But when I make a promise to survivors, I keep it.”

Hilton said over 50 million people have been victimized by sexually explicit deepfakes. “For me personally, strangers have created over 100,000 AI-generated explicit images and videos portraying me in the most graphic and disturbing ways imaginable,” Hilton said. “If this can happen to someone with my resources and my platform, imagine how powerless a young girl might feel facing this completely alone.”

Hilton’s comments were followed by Elliston Berry, an advocate who said a classmate made an explicit deepfake image of her friend group “from an innocent social media post” when she was 14, and distributed it throughout her school. “In a matter of seconds, my sense of security disappeared,” she said. “For me, the Defiance Act means recognition that victims deserve to be heard, that their experience matters, and that their right to seek justice should not depend on how quickly technology advances.”

American to Renovate, Expand Phoenix Lounges

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

American Airlines plans to refresh and expand its three Admirals Club lounges at Phoenix Sky Harbor International Airport.

The carrier said Tuesday that it will add seating, upgrade amenities, and improve food and beverage service across all three spaces in Terminal 4.

In the near term, the Admirals Club lounge in Concourse A between gates A19 and A21 will get a new servery area, infrastructure enhancements to support expanded food and beverage offerings, and facility updates to improve the overall customer experience.

Longer-term plans call for nearly tripling the combined footprint of the Concourse A lounges, the other being above gates A7-A9. New furniture and finishings will be brought in, and seating capacity will expand by 150%.

The lounge in Concourse B, between gates B5 and B7, will get a new self-service food and beverage area and have its seating capacity increased by 20%.

“Phoenix continues to be an important hub in our network, and these investments reflect our commitment to giving customers more space, comfort, and convenience when they travel through PHX,” American Chief Customer Officer Heather Garboden said in a news release. “While we look forward to the development of the new West Terminal, these enhancements will help create an even more seamless travel experience for the millions of customers who pass through Phoenix each year.”

The planned West Terminal will be built on the west side of the airport between Terminal 3 and Taxiway U. American is expected to serve as the terminal’s primary carrier.

According to the airport’s timeline, the project should be finished by 2033.

Savvas Learning Company Named to the 2027 GSV 150

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For the fifth year in a row, Savvas is recognized among the world's most transformational growth companies in learning and workforce skills

PARAMUS, N.J., Nov. 18, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Savvas Learning Company, a next-generation K-12 learning solutions leader, is proud to announce that it has been named to the 2027 GSV 150, earning recognition as one of the world's most transformational growth companies in learning and workforce skills. This is the fifth year in a row that Savvas has been named to the GSV 150.

At Savvas, our instructional philosophy is built upon delivering flexible educational resources from pre-K-12 to workforce learning.

"At Savvas, our instructional philosophy is built upon delivering flexible educational resources, from pre-K-12 to workforce learning, that empower teachers and improve student outcomes," said Bethlam Forsa, CEO of Savvas Learning Company. "Savvas is honored to be recognized on the GSV 150 as it demonstrates our commitment to using technology to advance personalized learning and data-driven instruction."

GSV is a global community and investment platform focused on elevating the scope and scale of innovation in the education and workforce skills sector. The GSV 150 recognizes category leaders building at scale, expanding access, and creating new possibilities for how people learn, grow, and thrive.

Savvas is a longtime leader in providing the highest quality print and digital instructional materials that meet the needs of today's pre-K-12 classrooms. Its portfolio of engaging, rigorous, standards-aligned learning solutions span all grade levels and disciplines, including award-winning math, literacy, science, and social studies programs, all accessible on the Realize digital platform. The company takes an intentional and innovative approach towards utilizing advanced technologies and developing AI-powered tools that support educators and promote student learning in a protected, safe environment.

Savvas is also helping to prepare students for both post-secondary education and career success with Savvas PathMaker, an all-in-one digital college and career readiness solution. Designed to offer flexibility for educators and choice for students, Savvas PathMaker delivers a comprehensive catalog of Outlier by Savvas dual enrollment and Savvas CTE career-focused courses so high school students can earn college credit, job-ready skills, and industry-recognized certifications before they graduate.

"At a time when the possibilities for learning and work are expanding rapidly, we're seeing companies break down traditional boundaries, bring together complementary capabilities, and find new ways to create meaningful opportunities," said Lucy Baldwin, vice president, product, GSV Summit. "As we consider what the world needs now, this year's GSV 150 demonstrates the value of bringing different ideas, expertise, and capabilities together to address the evolving needs of learners, educators, and employers."

I’ve Been Wondering If A Grand Theft Auto Movie Will Happen, And Someone Finally Asked An Exec

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The Wait For Daniel Craig’s Bond Replacement Has Been Frustrating, But Denis Villeneuve’s Comments Are Encouraging

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The hype is real, but what about a movie?

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The hype surrounding the long-awaited release of Grand Theft Auto VI has reached a fevered pitch, with reviews for the game universally saying it’s living up to that hype. The GTA series has consistently been one of the most popular gaming franchises of all time, and one of the most successful entertainment franchises on Earth. It’s also one of the only monster franchises that hasn’t been adapted as a movie or a TV show.

That’s always surprised me, because it seems ripe for a video game adaptation, right? Now, the CEO of Take-Two Interactive, the company that owns Rockstar, the studio that produces the games, was recently asked about a possible adaptation. His answer is actually kind of positive news, though hardly concrete evidence that an adaptation is on the way.

While appearing on The David Rubenstein Show, CEO Strauss Zelnick was asked about the possibility of a movie. He responded:

We're very careful about line extensions because this is a creative company, and our goal is to make the best entertainment on Earth.  Entertainment we’re always proud of. We would make a movie or a television show based on our intellectual property if our creative teams were passionate about it, if they thought that would somehow engage and delight our consumers. It's a high bar to do that, and, of course, there's always a risk of failure. So we don't rule it out, but we're very, very selective.

“Line extensions” are when business use existing products or intellectual property to expand how they can take advantage of markets, like, for example, video game movie adaptations. Take-Two, as Zelnick points out, has been very careful with how they’ve expanded their brands outside of video games. No franchise shows that more than GTA. It seems ripe for adaptation and really has been for decades, but they have yet to pull the trigger. They do seem open to it, though.

Like millions of other people, I’m still waiting for a few more hours to be able to play GTA VI, as we all have been for years now. Based on the glowing reviews, this could be the version of the game most likely to get adapted into a movie. Reviews have talked about the storyline in the new game, praising it for how well the story is incorporated into the game. It seems full of drama (and great music). Plus, there are two playable protagonists, a couple named Jason and Lucia.

This is the first time GTA has featured a female playable character, and it tells the story of the two, which to me sounds like the perfect basis for a possible film. While the characters in previous versions have been fun, these two actually sound like the most realistic characters, with a deep backstory together. That really leaves the option open.

It’s also worth mentioning that Zelnick, who took over Take-Two in 2007, has a history in the movie world as the former President of 20th Century Fox and overseeing hit movies like Home Alone and before that worked for Columbia Pictures Television. If anyone knows how to strike a good deal for Take-Two, it’s Zelnick. I would love to see it happen one day!

He has been in the publishing industry for almost two decades, covering pop culture – movies and TV shows, especially – with a keen interest and love for Gen X culture, the older influences on it, and what it has since inspired. He graduated from Boston University with a degree in Political Science but cured himself of the desire to be a politician almost immediately after graduation.

Skyrim's Most Iconic Region Is Making a Major Comeback

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elder Scrolls Online is heading north in 2027, bringing a familiar snowy destination to its expanding map

Winter is coming to Tamriel, and The Elder Scrolls Online is preparing to take players somewhere particularly familiar. One of the most memorable regions from The Elder Scrolls 5: Skyrim is making its way to the MMORPG, giving fans another reason to revisit the icy northern reaches of the continent.

Bethesda has revealed that Winterhold is coming to The Elder Scrolls Online in early 2027 as part of Season Three. The new major zone and accompanying storyline will expand ESO's Second Era map, bringing the snow-covered region into the online RPG for the first time.

While Bethesda hasn't shared many details about what players can expect to do in Winterhold, it has confirmed that more information is on the way. A special livestream dedicated to Season Three and the new adventure is planned for January 2027, with further details expected in the developer's December community letter.

Winterhold is a remote, snow-covered region in the northern reaches of Skyrim, overlooking the Sea of Ghosts. Its namesake city was once a prosperous settlement, but much of it was destroyed during the Great Collapse, a mysterious disaster that caused large sections of the town to sink into the sea. The region is perhaps best known for the College of Winterhold, a prestigious institution dedicated to the study of magic.

In Skyrim, players can explore the College and join its ranks, embarking on a major questline involving mysterious artifacts, dangerous magical experiments, and the Eye of Magnus. But there's more to the region than its famous school of magic. The surrounding landscape includes icy coastlines, snow-covered mountains, ancient Nordic ruins, caves, and settlements inhabited by hunters and other hardy residents.

Winterhold is a particularly atmospheric corner of Skyrim, blending ancient Nordic history, magical intrigue, and some of the game's harshest wilderness.

Exactly which parts of the wider region will be included in The Elder Scrolls Online remain unclear. However, ESO takes place centuries before Skyrim, giving players an opportunity to explore Winterhold during a very different period in its history.

Not Micron, Not Sandisk. This Is My Top Memory Stock for 2027.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

SK Hynix is bigger than Micron and Sandisk in the DRAM and NAND flash markets, which is why it has the potential to outperform them next year.

By Harsh Chauhan – Nov 18, 2026 at 2:01PM EST

Micron Technology and Sandisk have sizzled on the stock market in 2026. Both stocks have delivered impressive gains this year, despite witnessing a significant pullback in recent months amid the broader correction in tech stocks.

Micron stock, for instance, has gained 128% in 2026, as of this writing. Sandisk's gains are even bigger at 194%. Importantly, both memory companies can step on the gas once again in 2027, driven by the artificial intelligence (AI)-fueled demand for compute and storage.

However, I think that Micron and Sandisk could be overshadowed by South Korean memory giant SK Hynix (SKHY -1.23%) next year. Let's look at the reasons why.

SK Hynix sells both dynamic random-access memory (DRAM) and NAND flash memory. Counterpoint Research points out that the company controlled 29% of the DRAM market in the third quarter of 2026. It is second only to Samsung in this market. Micron, for comparison, has a 21% share of the DRAM market.

Meanwhile, SK Hynix's 19% share of NAND flash puts it ahead of both Micron and Sandisk, which have market shares of 15% and 11%, respectively. The South Korean company's healthy market shares in both DRAM and NAND flash explain why it reported phenomenal growth in Q3. SK Hynix's revenue surged 328% year over year to $79 billion, easily beating the consensus estimate.

The company's operating profit jumped 632% from the year-ago period and exceeded analysts' expectations. SK Hynix is well-placed to sustain such impressive growth. That's because the company has already sold more than half of its production capacity for 2027 and 2028 under long-term agreements with 14 customers.

Moreover, SK Hynix anticipates that memory demand will outstrip supply through 2030, indicating that the favorable environment fueling the company's stunning growth is sustainable. Also, SK Hynix is ramping up production of its next-generation high-bandwidth memory (HBM) chips, known as HBM4.

It points out that HBM4 has twice the bandwidth of the previous-generation HBM3E chips and 40% higher power efficiency. SK Hynix notes that HBM4 revenue and shipments will surpass those of HBM3E in the current quarter, which should bode well for the company's margins. That's because HBM4 reportedly carries a 24% premium per gigabyte (GB) over the previous generation, HBM3E.

What's more, SK Hynix controls a third of the global HBM market, higher than Micron's 18% share. This is another reason to buy SK Hynix, considering that the global HBM market's revenue could surge to a whopping $641 billion in 2030, according to Bloomberg Intelligence. The firm has significantly upgraded this guidance from its earlier estimate of $260 billion, noting that the average price of HBM will more than double over the next four years.

So, there is a strong likelihood that SK Hynix's revenue growth will outpace Wall Street's expectations.

SKHY Revenue Estimates for Current Fiscal Year data by YCharts

SK Hynix's lead over Micron and Sandisk in DRAM and NAND flash should ideally give it greater pricing power over its rivals. This probably explains why analysts expect SK Hynix's earnings per share to increase at a much faster pace in the long run.

SKHY EPS LT Growth Estimates data by YCharts

Also, SK Hynix's valuation is in line with Micron and Sandisk's multiples.

So, even though SK Hynix stock hasn't made a bright start on the U.S. stock market following its listing on the Nasdaq this year, it remains worth buying on account of its red-hot growth, cheap valuation, and the ability to outperform Micron and Sandisk. In the end, I won't be surprised to see this AI stock's fortunes turn around in the New Year, and buying it while it trades at cheap multiples seems prudent.

Fed officials expect more rate hikes, worry about AI impact on inflation, minutes show

📅 2026-11-18  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Federal Reserve officials expressed confidence in October that further interest rate increases will be needed to stem inflation, though they decided to hold off while gathering more information, according to meeting minutes released Wednesday.Policymakers put artificial intelligence at the core of their discussions, indicating that demand associated with the technology would be "a material source of inflation risk." More broadly, officials said "inflation pressures in the economy were broad based" and would warrant action, with "some" policymakers advocating for a quarter percentage point hike at the meeting. A couple participants even noted the possibility that a larger increase might be needed at the next policy meeting in December.The meeting summary said "almost all participants judged that, under their baseline outlooks for inflation and the labor market, further action to raise the target range for the federal funds rate … would likely be appropriate, in order to ensure a timely return of inflation to the 2 percent objective."In a CNBC interview Wednesday, Fed Governor Christopher Waller reiterated his belief that the Federal Open Market Committee should consider a half-point hike in December.

Officials at the meeting expressed a high level of concern over AI, both as a source of inflation and financial instability. Fed staff members who briefed participants noted "that off-balance-sheet leasing commitments and circular financing arrangements among AI-related firms could amplify losses if returns on AI investment were to disappoint.""Most participants observed that AI-related debt had grown rapidly and that much of this growth had taken place through complex and opaque financing structures," the minutes said. "A few participants remarked that AI-related exposures were becoming increasingly concentrated among a small number of firms."Officials said they still expect inflation to fall off sharply in 2027 and beyond as the impact from the current shocks wear off.Ultimately, the FOMC voted 10-2 to keep its benchmark overnight borrowing rate locked in a range between 3.75-4%. Governor Lisa Cook and Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack dissented in favor of a quarter-point increase. Those voting in favor of the hold "pointed to benefits in accumulating additional information, which would help in determining the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal."

Since the meeting, the inflation data has showed that the pace of price increases remains well above target.

The consumer price index in October rose 0.4% for all items and 0.3% for the core that excludes food and energy. Both readings were 0.1 percentage point higher than expected.

While indicating concern over how stubborn inflation has been, most Fed officials who have spoken publicly advocated a patient approach.

However, Waller said Wednesday that the Fed may have to "shock" markets by approving a half-point hike in December. He is the only central banker to espouse that belief publicly, though St. Louis Fed President Alberto Musalem said he anticipates as much as a full percentage point move higher by the spring of 2027. As governor, Waller is a permanent FOMC voter; Musalem will not vote until 2028.

Chicago Fed President Austan Goolsbee said Friday on CNBC that the central bank risks losing its credibility if it doesn't act.

At the core of policymakers' concern is whether rising prices related to the Iran war and President Donald Trump's tariffs, in many cases temporary events that wouldn't prompt a response, are instead becoming more entrenched.

Markets are fully pricing in a quarter percentage point hike at the Dec. 8-9 meeting that includes low odds of a half-point move, according to the CME Group's FedWatch gauge. Futures traders also are pricing a solid likelihood of two more increases in 2027.

Christopher Nolan Reveals Why Watching 'The Odyssey' at Home Is Better than IMAX

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The Oscar winning director explains how repeat viewings reveal new layers in his cast's performances and shares his home theater tips.

The Oscar winning director explains how repeat viewings reveal new layers in his cast's performances and shares his home theater tips.

Christopher Nolan has spent much of his career championing the theatrical experience. From shooting on IMAX film to encouraging audiences to see his movies on the biggest screens possible, the director has made no secret of his belief that movies are meant to be experienced in theaters.

But that doesn't mean Nolan thinks watching a movie at home is a lesser experience. In fact, the filmmaker believes home viewing offers some advantages that even the most impressive theater can't replicate. And with The Odyssey arriving on digital, 4K Ultra HD and Blu-ray on Nov. 17, he's encouraging audiences to take advantage of them.

Speaking with Polygon ahead of the film's home release, Nolan discussed how watching a movie in your living room can change the experience, particularly when it comes to appreciating the work of the actors.

"The fun thing about watching a film in the home is a different kind of concentration," Nolan tells Polygon. "When you go to a movie, you're in the dark, you're really focused for a couple hours. In the home, it's a different type of concentration because you can pause the movie, you can go get a snack, come back."

That freedom means viewers engage with a movie differently than they would during a theatrical screening.

"It does allow you to control the timeline and really look at the detail of things," he said. "And for me, it's really about the performances."

While The Odyssey features enormous ships, mythical creatures, and elaborate action sequences, Nolan believes the film's greatest rewards on repeat viewings may come from paying closer attention to its actors.

"The human element is what connects you with the story," Nolan says. "But I think the work of these actors, this cast, this incredible cast in the film, it rewards multiple viewings."

Nolan knows that better than most, having spent countless hours reviewing footage during the editing process. He pointed to the layers in performances by Matt Damon, Anne Hathaway, Tom Holland, Robert Pattinson, and Samantha Morton as details that audiences might appreciate more fully when watching again.

"There's subtleties and nuances in what they're doing that I, in the edit suite, get to watch again and again and again and sort of pick up on all the different things that Matt or Anne or Tom or Rob, what they're bringing to things, or Samantha Morton," Nolan says. "It's really fun for people to be able to look at that in the home and see all the different layers that these guys have put into the story."

When I mentioned that my wife and I enjoy discussing movies while watching them at home, something that isn't exactly encouraged in a theater, Nolan acknowledged the experience has its own appeal.

"It's a different form of engaging with the story, and it has strengths and it has weaknesses," he says. "Nice to be able to sit on your own couch and stretch out, you know what I mean? It's been really exciting over the course of my career to see the leaps forward in terms of technology, in terms of what you're able to do in the home."

Nolan also appreciates how much home-viewing technology has improved during his filmmaking career.

"It's been really exciting over the course of my career to see the leaps forward in terms of technology, in terms of what you're able to do in the home," he says.

And for anyone interested in bringing a little more of the theatrical experience into their living room, Nolan has a suggestion.

"I'm a big fan of projectors," he said. "You can get a relatively low-cost, pretty high-quality projector, darken your living room, maybe introduce some popcorn into the equation or whatever. Have a mini movie theater and do it on your own timeline. That's tremendous fun."

But Nolan doesn't believe a movie necessarily loses its theatrical identity once audiences begin watching it at home. In fact, he suggests that the excitement surrounding a major theatrical release can follow a film into people's living rooms.

"When you have a movie that's played, as The Odyssey has, at length in movie theaters, even viewed in the home, there's a kind of excitement that comes with an event like that," Nolan says. "Even if people are seeing it for the first time, I think the film sort of carries that with it."

The Odyssey arrives on digital, 4K Ultra HD and Blu-ray on Nov. 17

An AI Debt Collection Service Exposed 6.6 Million Accounts and 315,000 Call Recordings

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Two vulnerabilities in the India-based AI-driven debt collection platform CreditNirvana exposed 6.6 million accounts and about 315,000 call recordings from the company's agentic call center, according to research released on Wednesday. Owned by the Indian fintech company Perfios, the platform is built to “read a borrower’s full context, decide the next best action, and execute it autonomously, continuously, and within policy.”

The bugs were patched in CreditNirvana's systems in June and were disclosed publicly in October. But independent researcher Eaton Zveare, who found and disclosed them, notes that, while the vulnerabilities were active, they exposed personal information on millions of borrowers. Details about financial collections are inherently—perhaps obviously—sensitive, because they relate to unpaid accounts or debts and often involve extensive documentation of people's personal histories.

“They fixed it in one day, which was great, but this is really a pretty significant case,” Zveare tells WIRED. “Some of the largest fintech firms across India use this company to autonomously collect debts, so they naturally hold data, including credit scores, call recordings, transcripts, court records, and other sensitive documents. There’s just so much in there that makes it a very sensitive issue.”

CreditNirvana and Perfios did not return WIRED's requests for comment. Zveare says he worked directly with India’s Computer Emergency Response Team (CERT-In) to get the vulnerabilities fixed. CERT-In categorized one vulnerability as “high” severity and the other as “critical” severity.

The high-severity vulnerability impacted the call center data, exposing hundreds of thousands of calls as well as recommendations for next steps generated by the platform after calls were complete. It could also have been exploited to allow an attacker to manage AI agents within the platform and direct their own agentic campaigns.

The critical severity vulnerability impacted CreditNirvana's main platform, and Zveare says it specifically exposed the data of three corporate customers—all prominent Indian financial services firms—using CreditNirvana for their collections. The data also exposed millions of individuals’ accounts, which contain credit reports, ID cards, case management details, and legal documents, along with certain types of communications, including millions of emails, texts, and call transcripts related to financial services, unpaid accounts, and collections. The vulnerability also could have been exploited by an attacker to track the location of human debt collection agents and for some administrative privileges within the system.

“Successful exploitation of this vulnerability could allow the attacker to enumerate platform users, establish sessions as arbitrary users including administrative users, access and modify sensitive information with the privileges of the impersonated user,” CERT-In wrote.

Zveare says he is relieved that the issue was resolved quickly, but is conscious that the bugs were potentially present in CreditNirvana's systems for years. As AI is integrated into more and more automated systems, synthesizing data from a greater number of sources and using it to draw conclusions, Zveare says, the episode serves as “an important example of an AI-powered service having a very sensitive data exposure.”

Nobody Prepared 4DX Audiences for Olivia Colman Having Sex With a Basket

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The seat-shaking theater tech is supposed to make audiences feel part of the action. But then came 'Wicker.'

The seats move. The wind blows. Water sprays you in the face. That’s 4DX, the movie theater format designed to make audiences feel like they’re inside the action. Usually, that means a chair-shaking car chase or superhero battle. For those watching Wicker, it meant being bucked in their seats every time Olivia Colman had sex with a basket.

The dramedy about a fisherwoman who commissions a basketmaker to weave her a husband became the first romance to get the treatment since the format was developed in 2009. Applying that technology to Wicker was “a special case,” says Erik Dane, vp digital marketing at CJ 4DPLEX, the company behind the format, because “in the world of 4DX, there are not that many erotic scenes.” Related Stories Movies 'Wicker' Star Alexander Skarsgard Will Be Pushed Differently for Golden Globes Than Other Awards (Exclusive) Business Alexander Skarsgard to Narrate Quinn Audio Erotica Original Set in World of 'Wicker'

Judging by one viewer’s account, they’ve figured it out — even if it’s murder on the janitorial staff. “The seats start thrusting,” posted Tumblr user cranberrymoons in her online review, describing how moviegoers were “covered in the popcorn and diet coke … flying everywhere.” When the onscreen couple broke the bed, the seats jolted along. “My hair is in my face and I’m holding onto the arm rests of my seat trying not to fall out,” she wrote. Afterward came “a beat of stunned silence … while everyone catches their breath.” Then, “the whole room starts cheering and clapping.”

The 60 participating theaters sold out for the Oct. 28 event (it was extended through Nov. 4). A Nov. 16 showing of two episodes of Heated Rivalry, HBO’s romance about two gay hockey players, quickly sold out, too.

He Lives in Madonna’s Old Mansion. Now He’s Made a Movie About Cambodian Genocide

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Taz F. Stark used to run the hottest club in London. Today, he’s a first-time director whose harrowing short film is wowing folks like Al Pacino, Bill Nighy and Rufus Swell.

By Benjamin Svetkey and Julian Sancton, Editors

Taz F. Stark has always known how to get a bunch of movie stars into the same room. He’s the former London nightlife impresario who in 2017 opened Laylow, the red-hot Notting Hill club where Rihanna partied, Mick Jagger unwound after a Stones show and Robert Downey Jr. privately screened an Avengers movie.

But now Stark, 41, is pulling off something far more impressive: He’s getting movie stars to watch his movie about the Cambodian genocide. His directorial debut, a harrowing 14-minute short called Child of the Rouge, has been earning raves from A-list fans like Al Pacino, who screened the film for friends; Rufus Sewell, who recently introduced it at a showing at the British Consul-General residence in Hancock Park; and Bill Nighy, who’s lined up for a London screening. Related Stories Music Boy George on His Controversial Israel Trip, Ed Sheeran and the Friends He Lost Along the Way Movies Basque Cinema Expands Its (Rapidly) Growing Footprint in Tallinn

“Directing and the hospitality business aren’t all that different,” Stark tells Rambling. “In both, you’re managing crazy people. But it has a similar magic. You gather a team of creatives, and they all get together and make something.” In this case, that all-star team includes exec producer Brady Corbet (Oscar-winning director of The Brutalist), Dhani Harrison (George Harrison’s son, who helped compose the score), and Olivia Neergaard-Holm (editor on The Apprentice). “My gaffer did The Big Lebowski,” Stark adds. “My AD was straight off CSI.”

Stark, who grew up in Britain, spent a chunk of his 20s teaching English in Cambodia, which is what inspired the film. He wrote the screenplay — it follows a couple delivering their baby in an abandoned hospital during Khmer Rouge’s seizure of Phnom Penh in 1975 — on a flight from London to L.A., where he now lives with his girlfriend, actress Stacy Martin, in Madonna’s old castle above the Hollywood sign. Soon after landing, he was introduced — by his dog — to the guy who’d produce the picture. “I was at this coffee shop, and our dogs started playing,” he recalls of his first meeting with Joe Russell, whose credits include the Marisa Tomei-Minnie Driver short Laboratory Conditions. “It was canine intervention.” Before long, they were shooting in a warehouse in Los Feliz decked out as a Cambodian hospital, with François Chau (Lost) and Pisay Pao (Z Nation) as the leads.

“The biggest difference between directing and hospitality,” Stark notes, “is that with restaurants, all your staff want to be doing something else. They all want to be writing and directing and acting. When you’re on a film set, everybody’s just grateful for the job they have.”

Stark’s film starts streaming on Mubi on Dec. 7.

Will the Kennedy Center Honors Go Full Trump?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The once star-studded D.C event is moving to new digs next month. Will the president’s name come along?

By Benjamin Svetkey and Julian Sancton, Editors

Never mind who’s onstage at this year’s Kennedy Center Honors. Keep an eye on the banners.

Donald Trump’s efforts to put his name on the performing arts institution continue to be thwarted by a federal court. But this year, while the Kennedy Center’s main building is closed for renovations — and still, after five months, remains shrouded by a tarp hiding the building’s original signage — the Dec. 7 honors ceremony is moving to Washington’s Capital One Arena, where the judge’s no-name-changing order may not apply. Related Stories Business Trump Says Federal Film Tax Credit Is Gaining Momentum Movies Mind the Gap: British Producer-Financier Phil Hunt Is Bringing Head Gear Stateside

“The injunction may only block Trump from affixing his name to the physical building that’s owned by the federal government,” says a source familiar with the Kennedy Center’s inner workings, noting that it remains “a very good question” as to whether there will be “Trump-Kennedy Center” branding at the Capital One event. After all, we know how much the president loves top billing.

If Trump’s name does end up on posters and programs, it’s unlikely the recipients will object too strenuously. This year’s crop of honorees — David Mamet, Paul Anka, David Foster, Andy Garcia and Barbara Mandrell — ranges from outspoken Trump admirers to, at least publicly, Trump agnostics. Mamet has enthusiastically championed the president; Anka performed a modified version of “My Way” at Mar-a-Lago (“When we vote your way”); and Foster hosted a fund-raiser for Trump-backed L.A. mayoral candidate Spencer Pratt.

“In normal times, the board was 50-50 Democrat and Republicans,” says the source. “But Trump is now chairman of the board, so he’s making the final decisions.”

Also in normal times, honorees were traditionally announced in midsummer. But this year, like last, they came months later. The holdup in 2025 was apparently because of Tom Cruise, who kept the board dangling while considering the invitation before finally deciding he had “scheduling conflicts.” It’s unknown who, if anyone, kept the board hanging this year, but they still haven’t announced a lineup of presenters and performers. Those names could provide an even more revealing measure of Hollywood’s willingness to participate in the Trump-Kennedy Center Honors — or whatever they end up being called.

‘Furious’ Creator Elizabeth Meriwether to Receive ATX TV Festival’s 2027 Showrunner Award

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Elizabeth Meriwether is set to receive the 2027 Showrunner Award at next year’s ATX Television Festival, Variety has learned.

Meriwether will take part in a conversation onstage at the festival, which runs June 3-6, 2027, in Austin, TX. It will be her first time appearing at the festival.

The Showrunner Award is meant to recognize those who “foster an environment of collaboration and creativity in their role as showrunner, and whose creative voice has a singular and expansive influence on TV,” per the official description. Past recipients include David E. Kelley and Bill Lawrence. Related Stories UFC 334 Livestream: How to Watch Gane vs. Hokit Online Here's How to Watch the Troy Trojans vs. South Alabama Jaguars Game Live Online

“ATX TV Festival is such a special gathering of people who truly love television and truly love Texas BBQ, and I am deeply grateful for this honor,” Meriwether said.

Meriwether most recently created and served as showrunner on the Hulu series “Furious” which starred Emmy Rossum as an FBI agent hunting a serial killer played by Lola Petticrew. The show was loosely inspired by the 1987 film “Black Widow” and was renewed for a second season in August.

Meriwether is also known creating the hit Fox single-cam comedy “New Girl,” which ran for seven seasons. She is also behind the Emmy-winning Hulu liminted series “The Dropout” starring Amanda Seyfried, which delved into the life and career of Elizabeth Holmes. Her other credits include the acclaimed series “Dying for Sex” at FX.

“From the pure joy of ‘New Girl,’ to the righteous anger of ‘Furious,’ to the sharp surreality and cultural relevance of ‘The Dropout,’ there are few writers in television that are willing – and able – to consistently challenge our expectations in the way that Liz’s stories do,” said Jenn Morgan, head of programming for the ATX TV Festival. “As a showrunner, she continues to prove that creative risks are worth taking, and that collaboration is essential to producing TV that stands the test of time. We’re thrilled and honored to have Liz join us next summer, and to celebrate her remarkable career thus far.”

Comedian Morgan Jay Launches ‘Music Therapy’ Show With Guests Kam Patterson, Josh Johnson and More (EXCLUSIVE)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Morgan Jay, the stand-up comedian best known for his viral auto-tuned crowd-work songs, has launched a new show on YouTube called “Music Therapy.”

The interview series, which debuted on Wednesday with first guest Kam Patterson (“Saturday Night Live”), is part talk show, part improvised jam session. In each episode, Jay inhabits the role of a musical therapist, blending unscripted, intimate conversation with games and live songwriting. At the end, each guest will create a song or jingle with Jay. Related Stories 'Vote for America 2026:' Here's How to Watch Newsmax's Midterm Election Special Online for Free MS NOW Has 2026 Midterm Elections Coverage: Here's How to Watch Election Night Results Live Online Free

Following Patterson, the season lineup includes comedian Ashby (Nov. 25), rapper Yung Gravy (Dec 2.), “Dimension 20” game master Brennan Lee Mulligan, “Love on the Spectrum” star Abbey Romeo, actor and author Mara Wilson, comedian Mark Smalls, Los Angeles Chargers safety Tony Jefferson, impressionist Matt Friend and comedians Josh Johnson, Ron Funches and Mandal.

“It’s cool to finally get to a point in my career where I can make cool things with my friends that show who I am. And part of who I am is who I surround myself with,” Jay said in a statement. “I couldn’t be happier to work with Hello Party on this. We have such a great collaborative team, and I know we will make something new and unique in the space.”

“Music Therapy” is developed and produced by Austin-based creative agency Hello Party and financed by Live Nation Studios, which also provides access to its talent roster for guest appearances.

“The industry is changing, and agencies that want to stay relevant in the creator economy are going to have to pivot toward actually being creators,” said Ty Stafford, Hello Party’s founder and CEO. “‘Music Therapy’ is proof that branded entertainment can start with a great idea and, with the right partners in place, become something audiences actually want to watch.”

“At Live Nation Studios, we’re always looking for new ways to bring music and comedy together, and ‘Music Therapy’ is exactly the kind of original, creator-driven content we’re excited to branch into,” added Ryan Kroft, head of film and television at Live Nation Studios. “Morgan is a one-of-a-kind storyteller and partnering with him and Hello Party gives us an opportunity to bring that creativity to an even bigger audience as a digital series.”

“Music Therapy With Morgan Jay” was created by Jay and Stafford and is directed by Stafford. Brooke Schley serves as showrunner and co-writes the series with Jay. Executive producers are Stafford, Graham Sicalowski, Robin Rapino, Stella Bulochnikov, Brooke Schley and Matt Sadeghian.

Jay was named one of Variety‘s 10 Comics to Watch in 2025. He has become one of the fastest-growing comedians in recent years, amassing more than 20 million followers across social platforms. That virality has translated to hundreds of thousands of tickets sold and a recent comedy special, “Live at the Village,” which is streaming on Hulu.

As an actor, Jay’s credits include “Love Language,” “Stick,” “St. Denis Medical” and “Wonder Man.” He’ll next appear opposite Pete Holmes in the indie feature “The Big Kill.”

Rose Byrne To Head 2027 Tropfest Jury

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Rose Byrne will head the jury at the 2027 Tropfest.

The long-running short film festival will take place on February 13, 2027, in The Domain in Sydney. Byrne previously served as the Tropfest jury head in 2017.

This year, the Tropfest films will stream globally on YouTube. The festival will feature as a week-long cultural event titled TropWeek. Programming across the week includes a three-day educational program at the National Institute of Dramatic Art (NIDA) exploring the craft and business of filmmaking, supported by Screen Australia and YouTube. Related Stories News Broadway's 'Fallen Angels' Live Capture Starring Rose Byrne & Kelli O'Hara Sets Theatrical Release News Jenna Ortega & Rose Byrne Are Getting 'Nasty' For Warner Bros Clockwork & LuckyChap

The week culminates in a free day-to-night festival in The Domain on February 13, with live entertainment, followed by the evening screening of 16 world premiere shorts. Competition winners will be announced live on the night and via the YouTube livestream. Watch on Deadline

Tropfest film submissions open on Tuesday 1 December and close on Tuesday 5 January 2027. Filmmakers are required to create a short film of seven minutes or less featuring the 2027 Tropfest signature item: a necklace.

Prizes for winning filmmakers include a share of the CommBank Tropfest Emerging Filmmakers Fund’s $100,000. First, second, and third prizes will be $50,000, $30,000, and $20,000, respectively.

The Tropfest Foundation, a not-for-profit organisation launched in 2025, is led by a board comprising Chair Sarah Murdoch, Bryan Brown, Peter V’landys, Richard Weinberg and John Polson. Britt Wickes serves as Executive Director.

“I have such fond memories of watching great shorts at Tropfest with thousands of people under the stars in my hometown of Sydney,” Byrne said in a statement. “I’m thrilled to be back to lead the jury in 2027 and can’t wait to see what stories Australia’s next generation of filmmakers will tell.”

Revolut Lists GoMining Token for UK and European Users

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Exchanges » Revolut Lists GoMining Token for UK and European Users

The move brings the niche utility token to more than 15 million fintech app users under the EU’s MiCA regulatory framework.

Retail fintech giant Revolut has added GOMINING, the native utility token of the digital Bitcoin mining platform GoMining, to its in-app cryptocurrency trading service. The listing enables over 15 million Revolut crypto users across the United Kingdom and the European Economic Area (EEA) to buy, hold, and trade the token alongside everyday banking services.

Prior to this integration, GOMINING was primarily accessible through crypto-native exchanges such as Kraken, KuCoin, and Bitget.

The listing builds on an existing payment integration established in 2024 that allowed European users to fund GoMining accounts via Revolut Pay. It also adds GOMINING to Revolut’s cryptocurrency trading service.

Revolut reviews digital assets before listing them, assessing their risks, compliance requirements and potential financial crime exposure. It also monitors listed tokens on an ongoing basis as part of its procedures under the EU’s Markets in Crypto-Assets Regulation (MiCA).

“For years, there has been a clear line between crypto and traditional finance, and most people stayed on one side of it,” said Mark Zalan, CEO of GoMining, in a statement announcing the listing. “For GoMining, this listing means our token is no longer only for people who already live in the crypto world.”

GOMINING has several uses within the GoMining platform. Token holders can use it to pay digital miner maintenance fees, with discounts of up to 20%, buy digital miners on the platform’s marketplace and purchase boosts in Miner Wars, GoMining’s mining game.

The token also has a governance function. Users can lock their GOMINING tokens to receive veGOMINING, which gives them voting rights on the allocation of ecosystem rewards.

Revolut customers can now buy GOMINING directly through the app. To use the token on GoMining, they must first transfer it to an external GoMining account.

The Revolut listing gives GOMINING exposure to a wider retail audience through a mainstream financial app. It also provides another way for users to acquire the token without first opening an account on a crypto-native exchange.

However, buying GOMINING through Revolut does not provide direct access to GoMining’s platform features. Users must transfer their tokens to an external wallet before using them within the GoMining ecosystem.

Istruzioni per l'uso del calciomercato: dallo scouting al moneyball e l'AI, tutto quello che c'è da sapere

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Il calciomercato è il nostro core business, d'accordo. Ma è pure quel concetto astratto che tiene svegli i tifosi durante nelle fasi operative e che alimenta i sogni dei bambini. Dietro le notizie c'è però un mondo che molti non conoscono e che nella giornata di oggi proveremo a raccontarvi nel dettaglio, partendo dai metodi classici per acquistare un giocatore per arrivare ai nuovi modi di fare, sempre più creativi, di agenti e direttori sportivi. Spoiler: l'intelligenza artificiale ha fatto il suo ingresso prepotente anche in questo mondo.

Il mondo delle trattative ha funzionato così per decenni. Impersonificato dagli storici direttori sportivi italiani, basava il suo fondamento sui contatti personali dei vari dirigenti di club. Rapporti più o meno stretti con agenti e intermediari, rete di collaboratori in giro per il mondo e scouting. Tanto scouting. Alcune società ci hanno costruito sopra una fortuna, scovando sui campi di periferia talenti poi diventati icone mondiali. Concetti che esistono ancora ma che stanno piano piano scomparendo.

Nell'ultimo decennio il calcio ha incontrato i dati e le sfaccettature proprie del mondo finanziario. Mettendo tutto nel pentolone ne è uscito fuori il Moneyball, di derivazione dal baseball. In soldoni si tratta di andare ad individuare le inefficienze del mercato, comprando il "prodotto" sottovalutato e non il grande nome. Significa, semplificando ancora, comprare a 5 ciò che in realtà vale 20. In questo ha sempre più peso l'utilizzo dell'AI, con i suoi modelli predittivi che hanno fatto irruzione nelle stanze dei bottoni dei vari club, non solo di Serie A.

C'è poi chi si affida al momento storico per indirizzare le strategie. C'è chi qua e là nel tempo ha cercato di costruire degli "instant team", squadre che puntano su nomi e giocatori di conclamata esperienza per provare a vincere tutto e subito. Raramente questa strada ha funzionato, e infatti si vede sempre meno. O c'è chi scandaglia il panorama mondiale alla ricerca dei migliori parametri zero: un modo semplice per abbattere il costo dei cartellini in favore di ingaggi (e commissioni) più corpose.

MCNC Chief Operating Officer Tommy Jacobson receives Order of the Long Leaf Pine

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Broadband and networking pioneer honored with North Carolina's highest civilian recognition for more than three decades of public service and leadership

RESEARCH TRIANGLE PARK, N.C., Nov. 18, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- Tommy Jacobson, Senior Vice President and Chief Operating Officer of MCNC, today has been awarded the Order of the Long Leaf Pine, the most prestigious honor presented by the Governor of North Carolina for exemplary service to the state.

The award recognizes Jacobson's more than 32 years of dedicated leadership in expanding high-speed connectivity, closing the digital divide, and laying the technological foundation for North Carolina's schools, universities, health care systems, and rural communities.

His career of service began in the summer of 1993 when he joined MCNC â a technology non-profit established in 1980 â as a student intern prior to his senior year at NC State University. After graduating in 1994 with a Bachelor of Science in Electrical Engineering, he went on to embody the university's "Think and Do" ethos across three decades of executive, operational, and engineering leadership.

As the chief architect of the North Carolina Research and Education Network (NCREN), Jacobson oversaw the design, deployment, and operational resilience of middle-mile broadband infrastructure serving nearly 1,000 community anchor institutions. Today, the network spans more than 4,500 miles across all 100 North Carolina counties, delivering high-speed connectivity and cybersecurity services to millions of users.
